# Review log — 0107_fix_the-delegation-tree-s-budget-lifecycle-is-under-specified

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass 2, before the post-terminal dispatch redesign): pass 1 lifted the standing context from ledger entries spec.1.* through non-spec.1.*; its closure list is in the ledger and the archive. Pass 2 rewrote the Settled entries **D3 budget return context**, **Decision C settled**, **Checklist numbering**, **DOC-1 staged**, and **Tier-4 detection test** to the current proposal text, added the Settled entry **0106 writer set**, and deleted the Opens the current text answers: **CODE-2 metric emitter targets** (CODE-2 Targets name `IncDelegationBudgetKeysExpired`, `Options.IncDelegationBudgetKeysExpired`, and the `sessionsrv.go` wiring), **§8.3 step 3 wire code** (SPEC-3 Edit (b) names the permanent `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`), and **Runbook BUDGET_EXHAUSTED Trigger line** (summary defect **The delegation-budget-recovery runbook names surfaces that do not exist** takes the `/v1/delegations` and `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` wording). No deliverable changed and no ledger entry was deleted.

### Settled

- **Grant pool**: §8.6 extension grants form one tree pool on `delegation_tree_budget.ext_*`; `DenialStore.Grant` clamps each dimension to (effective max − root base − ext_now) under the row lock and returns the applied grant; the per-session delta, `StoreBudgetSource.Forget`, and its sweep share are deleted (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1; owner question closed by f1.human-decisions).
- **Grant scope home**: the §8.6 **Scope:** list is the one home for what a grant raises (requester's session limits only; token amount raises every parent's reservation ceiling = parent `maxTokenBudget` + row cumulative token grant; existing leases unchanged). **Extension state:** states only "A grant writes none of the §8.3 Redis counters." (spec.2.fix-G3.1, supersedes spec.1.fix-G1.1's "second home" rejection).
- **Tree-wide ceiling raise is shipped behavior**: `reserveScript` folds tree-wide `token_grant` into every parent's cap, and the tokens counter is tree-wide, so the pool is admitted once tree-wide (treebudget.go reserveScript; spec.2.review-security.1).
- **Effective max is per dimension**: code `effectiveMaxDims` / `dimCeiling(EffectiveMax, ParentCeiling)`, so Edit (c)'s "on each dimension" is grounded (spec.2.review-security.1, spec.6.review-mechanism.1).
- **ExtendLease response amounts**: `RaiseBudget` takes `res.grantedTokens`, so ExtendLease reports the applied (clamped) grant (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **`denialpg.Grant` today**: unconditional additive upsert on `ext_*`; nothing caps the cumulative grant (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **`delegation_tree_budget` row**: PK (tenant_id, root_session_id), no subtree_id, no FK to sessions; migration 0130 adds `ext_*` to it, so grants are tree-wide and SPEC-6's singular "row" is exact (spec.1.review-mechanism.1, spec.6.review-fresh.1).
- **autoModeRateLimit is Redis-backed (USEFUL x4)**: `w.rateLimiter` is `ratelimitredis` when Redis is configured, in-memory only in Redis-less dev; SPEC-4 Edit (e) ("on each gateway replica") was false and is deleted; §19 row 13's auto-mode clause stays (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **§11.2 reading (USEFUL)**: §11.2 enforces per-session budgets against shared Redis counters; in-memory per-session tracking happens only in the Redis fail-open window; F-11.2.31 files the process-local enforcer as a gap (spec.1.review-* lenses; spec.1.follow-up-fix.1 corrected D11 and Non-goals).
- **D11 wording**: the clamp caps the root's lease plus the cumulative grant at the effective max (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **SPEC-9**: §19 row 13 is SPEC-9; only its elicitation-mode clause changes (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Durability handoff sentence deleted**: SPEC-4 Edit (d) deletes "On coordinator handoff ... before accepting any new extension requests" outright; **Query semantics (handoff-safe):** is the single home of the per-request MUST read (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **Durability timing**: Edit (d) replaces the "periodic checkpoint transaction" clause with "before the gateway returns `REJECTED`, and a failed write fails the request with no grant instead of returning `REJECTED`" (spec.2.fix-G3.1, spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **CODE-7 item 4 (denial write)**: `Deny` runs while the batch is pending, before clearing `tc.pending` and closing `batch.done`; a `Deny` error becomes `batch.err` (non-spec.1.fix-G6.1).
- **CODE-7 item 3 (one lock)**: `treeConsent.mu` folds into the coordinator lock; the driver deletes `trees[root]` on close unless a success cool-off opened, then the AfterFunc deletes (non-spec.1.fix-G6.1).
- **Deny is tree-wide**: `MemoryBudgetSource.Deny` ignores the requesting session, so propagating a Deny error to all batched waiters is correct (non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1).
- **Testing item 6 Durability coverage**: tier 4 "A denial written on A is observed on B's first request" already exists; the `§8.6 (Durability)` annotation still resolves (spec.2.fix-design-G3.1, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Tier-1/2 clamp tests**: Testing item 6 carries a tier-2 leasedenial clamp case and a tier-1 concurrent memdenial Grant case (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **tier 9 `grantRecordingSource`**: implements `BudgetSource.ApplyGrant`; in CODE-6 Targets (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Sentinel MUST replaced**: SPEC-3 Edit (b) replaces the §8.3 MUST; exception for a marked first-reservation run; SessionStore witness; one unmarked rerun; "only a second `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` is a reservation detection"; `budget_return.lua` creates nothing; failed SessionStore or row read fails closed with `DELEGATION_BUDGET_UNAVAILABLE` (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Cleanup trigger line**: Edit (b) replaces "When the gateway receives `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` from either Lua script ..." with "On a reservation detection (above), ..." (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Race-free rerun (USEFUL)**: rerun follows the witness read, which follows the concurrent child-row commit, which follows its sentinel create; so exactly one unmarked rerun suffices (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **Reserve precedes child insert**: `reserveTreeBudget` runs before `buildChildSession`, so the first reservation's witness sees no delegated row (spec.2.review-applicability.1, spec.6.review-fresh.1).
- **Reservation-only detection**: only `budget_reserve.lua` detects; a return's `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` modifies no key, is logged, calls no handler, counts nothing (SPEC-3 Edit (c) item 2; CODE-3 item 4) (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Non-failable root**: a detection fails the root only from a state §7.2 gives a `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED → failed` edge (running, input_required, suspended); otherwise it cancels descendants and skips steps 3 and 4; Edit (d) replaces the "detection can occur in any state" paragraph (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Reservations under recovery roots happen**: children keep running and can delegate while the root is `resume_pending`, `resuming`, or `awaiting_client_action` (§8.10 item 2, §7.3) (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Cleanup item 1**: "the entire delegation tree — same mechanism as root session cancellation (§8.10 ...)" is replaced by "every non-terminal descendant of the root, whatever its `cascadeOnFailure` policy"; Edit (d)'s first sentence is deleted; CODE-2 3(b) drops ", ignoring cascade policy," (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **CODE-2 root writer guard**: allow-list `budgetKeysExpiredGuard` (spec.2.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-8**: §16.1 metric row and §16.5 alert row move to "per detection"; Edit (b) deletes the alert row's "(cascade cancel + root → failed)" (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1, spec.2.fix-G1.1).
- **Keys-expired metric unemitted**: `lenny_delegation_budget_keys_expired_total` is only catalogued (catalog.go); no Prometheus collector registers it and nothing increments it, and CODE-2 Targets own the registration (non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1); `FailureClassBudgetKeysExpired` exists (spec.1.fix-design-G4.1, non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1).

- **Runbook nonexistent surfaces filed**: summary unstaged-defects entry **The delegation-budget-recovery runbook names surfaces that do not exist**; DOC-1 stays scoped to the retired trigger and TTL wording (orchestrator.budget-return-decision; closes the non-spec.1.fix-design-G7.1 Open).
- **D3 budget return context (owner decision C, 2026-10-28; supersedes the owner-delegated order of redesign 1)**: summary D3 is the reason home, SPEC-2 the rule home, and CODE-3 item 1 the code home. CODE-3 has six items; the former item 7 (the gateway `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` reader) does not exist, because round 1 of the non-spec loop deleted it. The return's quiescence wait starts at the terminal transition and runs concurrently with the release. §8.3 step 1 (SPEC-2 Edit (b)) names `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` or `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` stream close, and step 2 the timeout from the terminal transition, but the consumer of `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` is deferred to remediation step R12 (summary defect **The gateway has no `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` consumer**), so until then the wait always ends at `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds`. Token crediting is unaffected by the deferral, because the return reads `SubtreeTokens` rather than the report. D3's CH-ATTACH early exit stays dropped; summary D3 gives the reason. The earlier closures stand: the full_revoke Trap and the `AfterRevokeWrite` defect carry no order, the under-read's home is the non-spec Edge cases bullet on the `SubtreeTokens` read, and the summary defect on `delegation.budget_return_usage_lag` covers the unemitted timeout path.
- **Decision C settled (owner, 2026-10-28)**: the return neither precedes the release nor waits for it to complete; a hung `Shutdown` cannot delay it past the timeout; decision B (startup refuses a quiescence timeout not less than the seal window in effect; pool admission has no check, per the summary defect **Pool admission has no seal window to compare**) keeps it inside the §7.1 terminal pipeline budget, so §7.1 needs no exception; it runs from the terminal pipeline, on the recorded root, once; a return cut by process exit is not reclaimed or retried. SPEC-2 Edit (b) rewrites §8.3 step 1 (wait from the terminal transition for `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` on `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` or its close; the release and the return do not wait for each other) and adds "of the terminal transition" to step 2. SPEC-10 edits §4.7.3 (the `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` row), §28.5.2 (the `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` **Messages.** bullet), and §29.4 (item 15) to drop the last-message-before-close timing. §28.8 gets no edit (orchestrator.owner-decision-C, current spec-changes text).
- **0106 writer set (D15)**: 0107's writer set follows approved proposal 0106, which lands first (summary D15; non-spec **Sequencing**). 0106 CODE-B item 3 deletes `expireSession` and restricts `failSession` to a locked `resume_pending` row (`resumePendingGuard`), reached from §8.10 tree recovery through `FailNode`. CODE-3 Targets and item 6 still name `expireSession` (orchestrator.compaction-before-dispatch-redesign; 0106 non-spec-changes CODE-B item 3).
- **Cut sweep bounded**: a crash after the root write is completed by the next detection or §8.10 orphan cleanup (spec.6.review-reliability.1, non-spec.1.review-reliability.1).

### Traps

- **Full_revoke carve-out (MISTAKE)**: round 1 put "a §11.4 `full_revoke` cancel write runs no terminal pipeline" into SPEC-2, turning the `AfterRevokeWrite` code gap into a spec carve-out against §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** (unconditional; §11.4 grants none). Deleted; the gap is a summary defects entry. Do not stage `returnTreeBudget` in `AfterRevokeWrite`; the reason is the summary defect **The §11.4 full revoke cancel writes run no terminal pipeline.** P5b (0095) is implemented and does not own this defect (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **Legacy-root predicate (MISTAKE)**: the draft keyed CODE-3/CODE-4 on `ParentSessionID` and CODE-6 on `RootSessionID`; a legacy derived root (parent set, depth 0, inherited root) would have been failed by `HandleBudgetKeysExpired` at its first delegation. Testing item 4 carries the discriminating case. Do not return to either field (non-spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Return order (MISTAKE, superseded by decision C)**: redesign 1 put the return before the release, which left §8.3 step 1 unreachable because the adapter sends `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` only in the `Shutdown` the release issues (`pkg/adapter/session.go` `emitFinalUsage`). Do not move the return back before the release, and do not make it wait for the release to complete (rejected: final tokens uncredited and four more spec files; a hung `Shutdown` consuming the budget) (orchestrator.owner-decision-C).
- **Cut-return statement, four attempts**: round 1 SPEC-2 sentence, round 1 Redis fold-in, round 2 deletion, round 3 reclaimer debate. Do not put any cut-return or "not retried" sentence into SPEC-2, spec Edge cases, or §10.1, and do not answer a recurrence with a reclaimer: a recount over-admits (reserve precedes insert), a membership set changes key types for one of four axes, §11.2 MAX never lowers leaked counters. A retried return after §11.2 rebuild would double-credit; CODE-3 item 4 keeps "the step continues" with no retry (spec.2.fix-G2.1, spec.3.fix-G2.1, spec.3.fix-design-G2.1).
- **`simple` preset stall framing**: the `maxParallelChildren 1` stall lasts until §8.10 post-settle cleanup, §12.8 erasure, or the TTL removes the keys; it is not forever (spec.3.fix-G2.1).
- **No root-state list in §8.3**: round 1 added a closed list (running, input_required, suspended) and it was false. Name the §7.2 `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED → failed` transition specifically; 0106's tree-recovery `resume_pending → failed` carries a different reason and must not satisfy the predicate (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Withdrawn option: new failed edges**: staging `→ failed (BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED)` for resume_pending/resuming/awaiting_client_action was rejected (resume-driver, CoordinatorFence, diagram cascades outside P4b). Stands while the non-failable-root design stands (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **`nonTerminalGuard` admits recovery states**: the root writer must not reuse it; `failSession` uses it for §8.10 unaffected (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **ListByRoot returns the root row**: any sweep over it must skip ID == root explicitly (CODE-2 3(b) does). Do not narrow `cancelNodeGuarded`'s guard instead; `cascadeToChildren` shares it and children legitimately sit in recovery states (spec.3.fix-G1.1).
- **Item 1 "same mechanism" stays deleted**: do not restore it or Edit (d)'s "The cleanup cancels the tree's non-terminal members". Do not edit §8.10's cascadeOnFailure paragraph or docs delegation.md:341; the sweep is a separate trigger like cancel_child and orphan cleanup (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Edit (d) anchor**: (d) replaces the original "The `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` detection can occur in any state ..." paragraph; do not bring back "The reservation detection can occur" anchor or a (c)-before-(d) dependency (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Cleanup ordering rule is gone**: "step 3 before step 1" existed only to stop cancelled children's returns re-detecting; with reservation-only detection it is removed from SPEC-3, the summary, and CODE-2's justification. CODE-2 still writes the root first, harmlessly (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Withdrawn option: widened return exemption**: "before §12.4 reconciliation restored the tree" was rejected (no restored witness; per-replica Reconciler misses short restarts). Reservation-only detection replaced it (spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **Durability sub-bullet**: no per-request read sentence or Query-semantics pointer goes back into Durability; "This ensures ... rolling update." still reads correctly; the old §11.1 link was a pre-existing miscite Edit (d) deletes (spec.1.fix-G2.1 as corrected, spec.6.review-citations.1).
- **No §11.2 precedent for replica-local state**: do not cite §11.2 as precedent for replica-local extension or session state (spec.1 lenses).
- **SPEC-4 Edit (e) stays deleted**: the auto limiter keys `lease_ext_auto:{tenant}:{root}` on the shared Redis counter (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Clamp inside the transaction**: compute the cap inside the denialpg Grant transaction or under the memdenial mutex; a pre-transaction TreeBudget read lets two replicas each grant the full headroom (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Sibling CEILING_REACHED is intended**: `ExtendForBudget` requests `EffectiveMax.Tokens`, so the first exhausting session takes the remaining headroom; do not refile without proposing a request amount (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Withdrawn option: per-session headroom**: durable per-session grants (sessions columns, lease JSONB, new table) rejected for new storage and cross-table transactions; owner closed it as tree pool (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, f1.human-decisions).
- **token_grant mirror is not a regression**: the tree-wide parent-cap raise carries existing behavior; do not file it as security (spec.1.review-security.1).
- **Per-session raise is replica memory**: the requester's raised limit lives in the applying replica's `grantedDelta` / `sessionbudget.Enforcer`; round 1 dropped "granted extension amounts" from Edit (c)'s in-memory exception list while still saying the requester's limits rise. Handoff is no worse than shipped (spec.2.review-citations.1, spec.2.review-performance.1).
- **Rerun race-safety depends on ordering**: if reserve stops preceding the child-row insert, a concurrent first delegation sees a delegated row and fails a new tree (spec.2.review-reliability.1).
- **Redis recovery code item**: do not add a reconciler CODE item, run_id/epoch detector, or `reserveTreeBudget` gate to 0107 without deciding the shared §12.4 restart detector; and do not restate "reconciliation rejects delegate_task" or "Restore rewrites the sentinel" as facts about code (non-spec.1.fix-G2.1, fix-design-G2.1).
- **Keep CODE-3's root warn-and-skip**: the `RootSessionID == ID` skip is the F-8.3.5 guard; do not add a skip for empty `ParentSessionID` (CODE-1 item 3 skips the missing per-parent key) (non-spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Orphaned parent id**: under the depth predicate, a child whose parent row was deleted has empty `ParentSessionID`; CODE-6's parent-ceiling read of an empty id is unspecified (pre-existing). `archiveSettledChild` still gates on `ParentSessionID == ""` (non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **SubtreeTokens must surface errors**: do not copy TreeUsage's swallowed `archive.Replay` / `tokens.Get` errors or its nil collapse for non-terminal descendants (non-spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Residual over-credit**: replica lag on GetMany, dropped best-effort `sessionusage.Add`, and direct-mode children under-read; D4's "never over-credits" holds for recorded consumption only; do not stage a primary-pool store without a finding (non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **No BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED wire code**: do not add it to errorclassify, §15.1, the proto enum, or the error catalog; unknown codes classify transient retryable (non-spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION details**: `errSessionTerminalState` sends nil details although the catalog documents `currentState`; pre-existing, copy as is (non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **sessiondeps.go stays in Files touched**: CODE-6 still edits it (non-spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **completeness_test.go**: builds the admin Router without `WithSessions`; adding it keeps the clear route registered and newly enumerates legal-hold routes; `leaseDenialStub` uses the old two-arg signature (non-spec.1.fix-G5.1).
- **Platform-admin clear assertion**: assert `ClearSubtreeDenial` receives the row tenant (`acme`) rather than the principal's (`platform`); a 204-only check passes a wrong implementation (non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1).
- **`resolveSuccessCoolOff` before the lock**: it does store I/O; under the single coordinator lock it would serialize every tree (non-spec.1.fix-G6.1).
- **`errBudgetSource` cannot drive the Deny case**: it fails `TreeBudget` too; the test needs a source where only `Deny` fails (spec.2.follow-up-fix.1's fixture was wrong) (non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1).
- **Generated alert files**: never hand-edit docs/alerting/rules.yaml, charts/lenny/files/alerting-rules.yaml, or pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml; DOC-1 regenerates (non-spec.1.fix-G7.1).
- **Runbook lines outside DOC-1**: runbook :32, :80, :81, :114 are wrong for their own reasons; do not pull them into DOC-1 (filed as a summary defect; see Settled) (non-spec.1.fix-G7.1).
- **Tier lists follow tests**: when a fix adds a test at a new tier, update the checklist step's tier list in the same edit (the CODE-6 step missed tier 2 once) (non-spec.1.review-applicability.1).
- **`ls` glob trap**: `proposals/NNNN*/*spec-changes.md` also matches `non-spec-changes.md`; filter with `grep -v non-spec` (spec.1.review-applicability.1).
- **Coordinator name collision**: §11.2 **Irrecoverable trees** "coordinating gateway replica" is the session coordinator, which still exists; §18 "serialized-per-tree elicitation queue" is not stale (spec.3.review-edit-sites.1, spec.6.review-edit-sites.1).
- **"No duplicate elicitation is sent."**: §8.6 flow step 2 stays absolute after SPEC-4 makes elicitation serialization per replica (spec.1.review-client-surface.1).
- **SPEC-5 repeats Edit (c)**: SPEC-5's closing sentence repeats "no grant to ... a terminal session" for queued requests (spec.1.review-single-source.1).
- **Pre-existing, do not file**: §8.3 perChildRetryBudget non-terminal return contradicts the terminal-only decrement (SPEC-2 agrees with the latter); §8.3 steps 1 and 4 describe budget_reserve.lua twice; §12.8 erasure table omits the delegation keys and row; no REC-1 record item is a plan-conformance gap (spec.6.review-mechanism.1, spec.2.review-single-source.1, spec.1.review-edit-sites.1, non-spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1).
- **Detached orphan after purge**: a detached orphan delegating after §8.10 deletes counters takes the detection path; its return takes the no-key path; both agree with SPEC-3 (spec.6.review-fresh.1).
- **failover SessionTotals**: reads only the Postgres primary and excludes Tier 1/2 buffered events (non-spec.1.review-performance.1).
- **credleasestore per replica**: a terminal write on a non-holder replica leaves the lease live; CODE-8's sweep clears the enforcer deny within one tick; already a summary defect (non-spec.1.review-security.1).

### Open

- **ListByRoot replica lag on the witness**: may miss a just-committed delegated row after a real expiry and restart counters via CreateTree; fix candidate is a primary read (non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1). non-spec.1.review-reliability.1 says replica lag is harmless for the first-reservation race because CreateTree uses SET NX; that covers a different case.
- **Testing item 6 tier 3**: "Tier 3 and tier 9 on the admin clear route" but handler tests are tier 1 and no tier3_contract dir covers it (non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1).
- **DELEGATION_BUDGET_UNAVAILABLE docs row**: Postgres-read cause has no docs home; kept out of DOC-1 as pre-existing (spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1, non-spec.1.fix-G7.1).

### Deferred

None open. Every ledger DEFERRED was closed by reconcile.1, non-spec.1.fix-G2.1, non-spec.1.fix-G6.1, or non-spec.1.fix-G7.1.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: closed the SubtreeTokens over-credit finding by deleting "and never over-credits" from summary D4 and adding one non-spec Edge cases bullet that points to §8.3 **Usage quiescence before budget return** and the summary defect **`delegation.budget_return_usage_lag` is never emitted** — BECAUSE the under-read has at least three sources (replica lag on sessionusage GetMany / sessionstore ListByRoot, best-effort sessionusage.Add errors discarded in cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go recordAccounting, and the unpulled final direct-mode poll interval), and only the declined FINAL_USAGE_REPORT quiescence wait closes all of them; §8.3 step 2 already accepts that the return "may slightly under-deduct usage" (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:426) — ALTERNATIVES: primary-pool reads for SubtreeTokens (closes only replica lag, needs new auxstores.go wiring plus treearchive.Cached primary instances); narrowing D4 to "over-credits only by dropped writes" (a closed enumeration that is already false); crediting zero tokens (leaves F-8.3.4 open).
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a guarantee that the token credit never over-credits, and do not enumerate the causes of the under-read in D4 or the Edge cases bullet. Any enumeration is incomplete (replica lag, dropped best-effort Add, final direct-mode interval), and a test author would encode it as a counter >= consumption invariant that the shipped design cannot meet. EVIDENCE: summary.md D4; non-spec-changes.md "## Edge cases and accepted failure modes" second bullet.
WATCHOUT: no test pins the over-credit, and Testing item 3 has no replica-lag case, by design. A later round that asks for a "lagging read pool still deducts" Tier-4 case is asking for the primary-pool mechanism this round rejected. EVIDENCE: review-log standing context "Read replicas" entry; pkg/gateway/session/sessionusage/pgstore/pgstore.go GetMany uses s.read.
OPEN: D3 ("reads the last-recorded sessionusage totals after the release") and the Non-goals quiescence bullet were not touched in this group; whether D3 should also acknowledge the under-read is for a later round.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]

DECISION: close the SubtreeTokens over-credit finding by deleting "and never over-credits" from summary D4 and adding one non-spec Edge cases bullet (the token credit deducts only the consumption the `SubtreeTokens` read sees, and the rest is credited back as unused; the bullet points to §8.3 **Usage quiescence before budget return** and the summary defect on `delegation.budget_return_usage_lag`). CODE-3 item 3, Testing item 3, the Builder wiring, and auxstores.go stay as they are — BECAUSE the under-read comes mostly from the missing §8.3 quiescence wait, which the summary already lists as a defect, and §8.3 already accepts that the return "may slightly under-deduct usage" in its degraded case. A direct-mode child's last 30 s poll interval (cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage.go defaultDirectUsagePollIntervalSeconds) is never pulled before the return, and best-effort `sessionusage.Add` drops (llmproxy_usage.go recordAccounting) are lost. A primary-pool read closes only replica lag — ALTERNATIVES: a primary-pool Builder (fixes one source of under-read out of three, and needs primary instances of sessionusage, sessionstore ListByRoot, and treearchive Replay, which is itself wrapped in treearchive.Cached); the reviewer's "over-credits only by dropped writes" wording (a closed enumeration that is already false, because the direct-mode final interval and replica lag are further cases); TokenDelta = 0 always (breaks the §8.3 step 3 credit and leaves F-8.3.4 open); a Tier-4 lag test (it pins only the rejected primary read).
WATCHOUT: do not answer this finding again by making the consumption read "more complete" (primary pool, a final billing flush, a direct-mode pull in the hook). Each of those closes one source of under-read. Closing all of them means implementing the §8.3 FINAL_USAGE_REPORT quiescence wait, which summary Non-goals declines — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:423-429; summary.md Non-goals "Gateway-side `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` quiescence".
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 WATCHOUT at review-log:663 "direct-mode children (no sessionusage rows)"]: direct-mode sessions do write session_usage. RecordDirectUsage calls recordAccounting, which calls sessionUsage.Add. It does so only once per poll interval (default 30 s, floor 10 s), so the final interval before the terminal write is unread. The comment in migrations/0158_session_usage.up.sql:19 that says the direct-mode path is "tracked separately" is stale — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:452,504; cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage.go:22,288.
FACT: every store SubtreeTokens reads is on the read pool: sessionusage pgstore GetMany, sessionstore pgstore ListByRoot, and treearchive pgstore Replay, which is also behind treearchive.Cached — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treearchive/pgstore/pgstore.go:120; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:868; cmd/lenny-gateway/auxstores.go:107,112.
OPEN: summary D3 says the stream close in `releaseAfterTerminal` "satisfies the §8.3 usage-quiescence step". For a direct-mode child the usage arrives by the periodic ReportUsage pull rather than on the stream, and nothing pulls on release, so D3 looks false for direct mode. This is a separate finding against D3; this group did not touch it.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the tree-budget return keeps the D3 position (after `releaseAfterTerminal`, before `expireRaisedElicitations`), and `returnTreeBudget` runs the `SubtreeTokens` read and the `Return` each on its own `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), treeBudgetReturnTimeout)` (5 s), copying `expireRaisedElicitations`; SPEC-2 gains the clause "and it runs even when the terminal pipeline budget has elapsed" — BECAUSE every hook step runs on `terminalContext` (budget = seal window + release allowance from TerminatedAt) and the release's adapter `Shutdown` can use up that deadline on a hung pod, which is the stuck child F-8.3.2 targets — ALTERNATIVES: accepting a second cut path in Edge cases (leaks the parent slot in F-8.3.2's own case); moving the return ahead of the release (reopens D3); one shared detached context (a timed-out read leaves the `Return` an expired context, so CODE-3 item 3's zero-`TokenDelta` fallback never lands); editing §7.1 "bounds the whole pipeline" (wider edit beside approved proposals' paragraphs).
FACT: `terminalContext` is `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), remaining)` with remaining = `TerminalPipelineBudgetFor(sealWindow, releaseAllowance)` less time since TerminatedAt; `releaseAfterTerminal` waits for `slotAccountLocks` on `s.lifetime()`; `Binder.Release` and the slot release make their accounting writes detached, so the budget cuts only `Shutdown`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go terminalContext, TerminalPipelineBudgetFor; terminal_hook.go releaseAfterTerminal doc comment; elicitation_expiry.go `elicitationExpiryTimeout = 5 * time.Second`.
WATCHOUT: `treeBudgetReturnTimeout` is a hard-coded 5 s constant like `elicitationExpiryTimeout`; code-best-practices.md asks for an override on non-spec defaults, but adding a flag is a new mechanism nobody requested. Do not add a flag without a finding. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/elicitation_expiry.go
WATCHOUT: `treeRoot` on a failed parent `Get` returns the deepest id it reached, which is the child's own id when the child's parent read fails; a depth-1 fixture (parent == root) cannot discriminate F-8.3.5. The Testing item 3 bullet uses a depth-2 child and asserts the archive row's `RootSessionID`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go treeRoot, archiveSettledChild (`root := s.treeRoot(ctx, sess)` feeds `Archive`).
FACT: the related-passages lookup for the SPEC-2 clause returned §7.1 Terminal pipeline context, §7.1 post-terminal window item 1, docs delegation-and-tasks Token Budgets, §8.3 item 3, and §29.4 step 15; each stays true (the post-terminal window is about the slot, which the return does not touch). — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md §7.1; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md §8.3.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: the CODE-3 return keeps its D3 position (after `releaseAfterTerminal`, before `expireRaisedElicitations`) and runs the `SubtreeTokens` read and the `Return` each on `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), treeBudgetReturnTimeout)` — BECAUSE a release whose adapter `Shutdown` hangs uses up the terminal context, and the stuck child that F-8.3.2 targets is exactly that case; the sibling step already uses this pattern — ALTERNATIVES: moving the return ahead of the release (breaks D3's usage-quiescence reason and still meets a slow seal/signals); accepting the cut as a second Edge-cases path (leaks the reservation in the case the return exists for); one shared detached context for read and Return (a read that times out also kills the Return).
FACT: `expireRaisedElicitations` already detaches from the terminal context with its own 5 s bound (`elicitationExpiryTimeout`), and Binder.Release and the keyed slot release make their claim and slot writes on a detached context, so only the adapter Shutdown is cut at the budget — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/elicitation_expiry.go:33; terminal_hook.go releaseAfterTerminal doc comment; podsession/reclaim.go detachedReleaseContext.
FACT: §7.1 says the budget "bounds the whole pipeline", but the shipped tree already runs listed-step accounting writes and elicitation expiry past it. SPEC-2 states the return's narrower rule with a link to §7.1, the same way SPEC-4 states its exception to §4.1 (refuted at non-spec r4 history line 61) — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116.
WATCHOUT: with the parent row's `Get` failing, `treeRoot` returns the deepest id it reached, which is the child's own id when the child's parent read fails. So the discriminating F-8.3.5 assertion is the archive row's `RootSessionID` and the Return's root equal to `sess.RootSessionID`, in a fixture where parent != root. The reviewer's phrasing "not the parent's id" is inexact — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:830-842.
WATCHOUT: if this loop's fixer may not edit spec-changes.md, the SPEC-2 clause becomes a DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: SPEC-2 lets §7.1's "bounds the whole pipeline" govern the return, but the return has to run after the budget has elapsed.

### [orchestrator.budget-return-decision]
DECISION: D3 settled (owner-delegated, 2026-10-28). The tree-budget return follows §8.3 **Usage quiescence before budget return** steps 1 to 3: a §7.1 terminal pipeline step after the CH-ATTACH stream closes or after `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds` from the terminal transition, whichever is first; it does not wait for `releaseAfterTerminal`; it runs within the terminal pipeline budget (for example under the release allowance), so §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** needs no exception. Cut-return Traps stand: no reclaimer, no retry, no failure sentence in SPEC-2.
DECISION: rejected the `WithoutCancel` detachment, the fixed 5 s `treeBudgetReturnTimeout` constant, and the §7.1 exception; SPEC-1's ", which the budget does not bound," insertion is reverted.
FACT: SPEC-1, SPEC-2, CODE-3 item 1, and Testing item 3's hung-`Shutdown` case carry one-line "pending redesign (D3)" notes; the rewrite of their ordering, context, and deadline text is left to the redesign pass.
FACT: `delegationbudget/pgstore/pgstore.go` `Write` ON CONFLICT updates only `tree_size`, `token_budget_consumed`, `tree_memory_bytes`, and `checkpoint_at` — EVIDENCE: pgstore.go `Write` upsert statement. Closes the checkpoint-overwrite Open as clean.
CORRECTS [standing Settled "Keys-expired metric unemitted"]: the metric is only catalogued; reworded.
DECISION: the runbook nonexistent-surfaces Open is filed as a summary unstaged-defects entry; no gateway route `/v1/delegations` or `/v1/admin/delegation-trees` and no `delegation.budgets` chart value exist — EVIDENCE: grep over pkg, cmd (Go) and charts/lenny/values.yaml returned no match.
OPEN: the full_revoke Trap, the summary `AfterRevokeWrite` defect, and the non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1 direct-mode OPEN reason from the retired "after the release" D3; the redesign pass restates or closes them.

### [redesign.1]
DECISION: applied redesign 1 (scratchpad/redesign/0107_*-redesign-1.md) Edits 1 to 22 in order; every anchor matched once and no edit was skipped. Redesigned areas: summary D3, What changes, Non-goals, and the usage-lag and full revoke defects; SPEC-1's step list (return before the release); the SPEC-2 block (ordering, timeout start instant, startup refusal); CODE-3 Targets and item 1 (sleep on the terminal context after item 2's checks, `Options.UsageQuiescenceTimeout`, startup check); CODE-5 item 1 (forget after `releaseAfterTerminal`); Testing item 3; non-spec Edge cases; Files touched; the Standing context full_revoke Trap, **Cut return accepted**, and D3 Settled entry.
DECISION: reconciliation added summary Open decisions A (no early exit, default applied), B (startup refusal, default applied), and C (return before release against §8.3 step 1, no default, blocks application), one Watch-out on the sleep's position, and the SPEC-2 deliverable-index wording. Checklist S13 already lists tier 1, so no step changed.
DECISION: deleted the four "Pending redesign (D3)" notes, CODE-3 item 1's `treeBudgetReturnTimeout` constant and `WithoutCancel` detachment, the "on a context that is not done" hung-`Shutdown` assertion, the Non-goals "once the stream closes" clause, and the "runs before the fan-out's binding release (D3)" reason.
WATCHOUT: the `CH-ATTACH` stream-close early exit lost because §8.3 step 1's stream is `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS`, which the gateway never opens, and `beginSealClosing` closes `CH-ATTACH` during the seal, so the exit fires before any usage settles. Do not reintroduce a binding-`Closing()` exit as its substitute (Open decision A Option 2).
WATCHOUT: the return after `releaseAfterTerminal` on a detached `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), treeBudgetReturnTimeout)` lost because it leaves the §7.1 pipeline budget and adds a fixed non-spec 5 s default; a hung `Shutdown` is now handled by running the return before the release.
WATCHOUT: a quiescence bound checked against the whole terminal pipeline budget lost because the sleep then consumes the release allowance that §7.1 says bounds the release; the bound is the seal window in effect (`sealSecondsInEffect`).
WATCHOUT: a sleep placed ahead of CODE-3 item 2's checks delays every session's release; the sleep runs only for an admitted delegated child.
OPEN: Open decision C. The related-passages lookup returned §8.3 step 1 and step 3, §4.7.3 `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT`, §28.5.2 **Timing.** and **Degradation.**, §28.8 `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS`, and §29.4 item 15 as sites the SPEC-2 ordering leaves wrong. They are not staged because Option 2 would make them correct as they stand; Option 1 stages them.

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2's quiescence bound is per pool ("a pool's `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` is not greater than `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds`") and is enforced at gateway startup and at pool admission, worded like the §5.2 claimOrphanTimeout sentence — BECAUSE a startup-only check against the largest pool lets a runtime pool PUT or DELETE break the bound and then blocks every replica's next restart; per-pool holds through create, update, and delete with no delete check — ALTERNATIVES: keep "largest of any pool" and refuse deletes (couples unrelated pools); a run-time clamp on the wait (silently undercounts, reopens decision B); adding a pool-level seal field (F-5.2.57's scope, owner P3).
FACT: the tree has no pool-level seal window. The only seal value is the gateway flag `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go, `sealSecondsInEffect` in cmd/lenny-gateway/startupgates.go), so the pool-admission half of SPEC-2 builds no code. — EVIDENCE: BUILD-GAPS.md F-5.2.57 ("None of the windows the bound names ... is a pool field: each is a gateway flag").
WATCHOUT: do not add a pool-admission check, a pool field, or a pool-admission test case to CODE-3 or Testing item 3. The gap is filed as the summary defect **Pool admission has no seal window to compare**, which widens F-5.2.57. The tier-1 startup cases stay correct, because an equal seal is "not greater" and is refused. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 1 and Testing item 3.
FACT: spec/05 §5.2 **Slot assignment atomicity** and spec/07 §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** still say "the largest `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` of any pool" for the budget. That stays true under the per-pool SPEC-2 bound and is not edited. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:678, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2's quiescence bound becomes per pool and is enforced at startup and at pool admission, mirroring the §5.2 `claimOrphanTimeout` sentence ("The gateway refuses to start, and pool admission refuses a pool, ...") — BECAUSE a per-pool bound survives pool create, update, and delete with no delete refusal, and every pool's seal exceeding the timeout implies the largest does — ALTERNATIVES: keep "largest of any pool" and refuse an update or delete that lowers the largest (rejected: refusing a delete couples unrelated pools); clamp the wait to the end of the seal window at run time (rejected: silently shortens the configured wait, under-counts tokens, and reopens decision B); add a pool-level seal field so admission has an input (rejected: F-5.2.57 scope, owner P3, cascades into the seal step and TerminalPipelineBudgetFor).
DECISION: the per-pool bound is stricter than the purpose needs (a pool with seal <= the timeout, default 5 s, is refused even when another pool has a long seal) — BECAUSE a seal window of 5 s or less permits at most one export attempt (initial backoff 5 s), so the cost is negligible and the rule needs no exception.
FACT: the tree carries `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` only as the gateway flag `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` (sealSecondsInEffect, TerminalPipelineBudgetFor); no pool struct or pool admission path reads a seal window — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/startupgates.go sealSecondsInEffect; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds; BUILD-GAPS.md F-5.2.57.
WATCHOUT: the pool-admission half of SPEC-2 has no input in the tree, the same gap F-5.2.57 files for the §5.2 sibling. CODE-3 must tell the implementor not to build a pool-admission check or a pool field; the summary defect bullet is the home that widens F-5.2.57 — EVIDENCE: BUILD-GAPS.md F-5.2.57; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/pools.go validatePoolForStore.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: add one replace-in-place to SPEC-4 Edit (d): in **Clock reference (UTC only):** replace "regardless of which replica is elected" with "regardless of which replica serves the request" — BECAUSE before SPEC-4 the extension path ran on the elected session coordinator (hence the Durability handoff sentence), and Edit (c) moves it to whichever replica serves the request; Edit (d) already rewrites "The newly elected replica" to "Any replica" in the adjacent sub-bullet on the same reasoning, so leaving "elected" in the Clock sub-bullet is inconsistent — ALTERNATIVES: leave it (rejected: the session-coordinator election still exists, but it no longer picks the replica that compares cool_off_expiry, so the phrase names the wrong selector); delete the trailing clause (rejected: the clock-skew sentence before it already carries the rationale, but a deletion and a replace are equal rungs and the replace keeps the cross-replica guarantee explicit)
CORRECTS [spec.6.review-edit-sites.1, spec.3.review-edit-sites.1]: the rejection of the §8.6 Clock-reference edit ("refers to session-coordinator election, which still exists") is wrong. The election still exists, but after SPEC-4 Edit (c) it does not decide which replica serves an extension, which is the selector the Clock sentence relies on. The §18 "serialized-per-tree elicitation queue" half of that rejection is not reopened here. Standing context **Coordinator name collision** (review-log.md) must drop "§8.6 Clock-reference and".

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds is a single gateway flag in code (`--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds`, default 300 s), although §7.1 calls it pool-level; so SPEC-2's "largest of any pool" startup bound compares one gateway value against `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds` (default 5 s) — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1175; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:114; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:426
FACT: §5.2's analogous terminal-pipeline bound is enforced at gateway start AND pool admission; SPEC-2's quiescence bound is startup-only. Not filed: whether the refusal exists is open decision B — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:678
DECISION: no findings under the performance/reliability lens — BECAUSE the new per-unit costs are small at Tier 3: one `delegation_tree_budget` PK read per delegate_task (settled), one replica ListByRoot plus one extra script run per new tree's first reservation (bounded by session-creation rate, ~30/s at Tier 3), three SessionStore reads per extension request (rate-limited), and replica-local consent state discarded after cool-off; Postgres failover already blocks delegate_task via the child-row insert, and a Redis outage already fails delegate_task closed per §12.4 — ALTERNATIVES: filing the 5 s pod/slot hold per delegated child before release (~8.3/s x 5 s ≈ 42 extra held pods against a 2,096 minWarm in the §17.8.2 Tier-3 example) rejected, because it follows from owner-settled D3 plus open decision A and minWarm is claim-rate based.
WATCHOUT: a return that stalls on store I/O (budget_return.lua during a Redis failover, SubtreeTokens replica reads) now runs before the release, so it eats release allowance; harmless unless the seal also used nearly the whole largest seal window (double fault). Do not file without a single-fault path — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116 (budget = largest seal + 30 s release allowance)

### [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no mechanism findings on the round-2 diff (SPEC-2 per-pool refusal sentence, SPEC-4 Clock reference replacement) or the rest of the staging — BECAUSE the per-pool predicate ("a pool's seal window not greater than q" refused) implies q < largest seal, so the wait ends before T + largest seal, where the §7.1 release allowance starts; Testing item 3's "equal refused, one second shorter accepted" matches "not greater than" — ALTERNATIVES: filing the parent-notified-before-slot-return race (signals precede the return's q-second sleep), rejected because no spec passage orders the return before parent notification (exhaustive meaning search over §7, §8, §29 returned 0 matches) and two spec.1 lenses already declined it.

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding on the spec staging in round 3. BECAUSE the new write and read load is small. The first reservation of a tree costs one extra Lua run plus one SessionStore ListByRoot. Each delegate_task costs one `delegation_tree_budget` PK read (already settled). A denial costs one synchronous Postgres write. SPEC-6 adds deletes only on erasure. None of this touches etcd, and grants serialize on the existing per-tree row lock. During a Postgres failover, delegate_task already stalls on the child-row insert, so the SPEC-3 Edit (b) fail-closed read is no worse. ALTERNATIVES: filing the pod or slot hold of up to `usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds` per delegated child. Rejected because summary open decisions A and C own the wait and the order.
WATCHOUT: placing the return before the release means the return's I/O (the SubtreeTokens read and the Redis Return, which have no timeout of their own) runs out of the release allowance whenever the seal uses the largest pool's whole seal window. A hung store call can then cut the release. This is the mirror of the hung-`Shutdown` risk D3 names, and it falls inside open decision C. Do not file it as a separate finding. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116 "largest `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` of any pool plus the **release allowance**"; spec-changes.md:40.
FACT: the SPEC-2 bound is per pool ("a pool's ... not greater than"). The §7.1 budget uses the largest pool. The per-pool check is stricter than "the wait ends before the release allowance begins" requires, but it is not wrong. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:40; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: the async boundary for the serial-sweeper stall sits in the sessionserver entry points `Server.OnSessionTerminal`, `expireSession`, and `failSession` (CODE-3 item 6), each running its hook on a goroutine on a detached context and holding a terminal claim from before it returns until the hook ends — BECAUSE every serial terminal loop (watchdog sweeps, orphancleanup expireOrphan, orphansession failOrphan, cmd deadlockFail, sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate for admin force-terminate and the §12.8 erasure and tenant drains, the budget terminator, and tree-recovery FailNode) reaches the hook through one of these three, and §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** and §12.8 *Drain* ("A successful drain does not wait for the session's terminal steps or the release of its pod") permit it — ALTERNATIVES: per-caller goroutines in five packages (spreads one rule, misses the drains); accept the stall (§8.10 60 s orphan cadence and maxTreeRecoverySeconds breached); goroutine inside AfterTerminalWrite/afterTerminalWrite/afterFailed or only around the return step (contradicts SPEC-2 "It precedes the pod or slot release" and pre-empts open decision C); delegated-child-only async (seal already stalls sweepers for every bound session); stamp-all-then-hook sweeper rewrite; bounded worker pool (no spec bound, cascadeToChildren precedent is uncapped).
WATCHOUT: the goroutine's claim must be open before the entry point returns. `expireSession`/`failSession` today `defer end()`; the fix moves `end` into the goroutine rather than ending it and reopening inside the goroutine, or WaitTerminalWork and startRelease see an unclaimed gap. Claims are refcounted, so OnSessionTerminal's extra BeginTerminal under the caller's own claim is safe. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go terminalClaimRecord.open; session_terminal.go expireSession/failSession; usage.go cascadeToChildren goroutine block.
FACT: `OnSessionTerminal` is a one-line call to `recordSessionCompleted`, which runs afterTerminalWrite then afterTerminalRelease inline on terminalContext; `afterFailed` calls AfterTerminalWrite inline. Only cascadeToChildren and mcptools cancelNode already run the hook on a goroutine. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go OnSessionTerminal, recordSessionCompleted; session_terminal.go afterFailed.
FACT: package-local fakes in watchdog, orphancleanup, and orphansession tests implement OnSessionTerminal themselves and are unaffected; the tests that break are sessionserver tests asserting hook effects when OnSessionTerminal/expireSession/failSession return, which CODE-3 Targets now name. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/*_test.go, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/*_test.go.
WATCHOUT: Edge cases bullet 6 ("the response of a writer that runs the post-terminal hook before it answers") stays true only for request-path writers after item 6; admin force-terminate no longer runs the hook before answering. Do not rewrite the bullet unless a finding shows a request-path writer is affected. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Edge cases.

DECISION: DOC-1 item 3 stages the configuration.md `pool.maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` Validation cell as a gateway startup refusal naming the flag `--delegation-usage-quiescence-timeout-seconds` — BECAUSE configuration.md has no `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds` row (doc-content.md bars an undefined term) and the tree implements no pool-admission check — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's literal cell text citing the undefined key; adding a new delegation-table row (pre-existing gap, and the chart cannot set the value); leaving "Must be > 0." (false after CODE-3).

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: the serial-sweeper stall is closed by a new CODE-3 item 6 that makes `Server.OnSessionTerminal` (usage.go) and the §8.10 tree-recovery writers `expireSession` and `failSession` (session_terminal.go) run the post-terminal hook on a goroutine on a detached context, holding a terminal claim from before they return until the hook ends, as `cascadeToChildren` does. BECAUSE `OnSessionTerminal` is the one adapter that every serial caller reaches (watchdog `recordCompleted`, orphancleanup `expireOrphan`, orphansession `failOrphan`, the cmd `deadlockFail` closure, `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` for admin force-terminate and the §12.8 erasure and tenant drains, and the budget terminator), so two sites in one package reach every loop and no sweeper package changes. ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's per-caller goroutines (five packages, about nine sites, and it leaves the §12.8 drains, also serial through `ForceTerminate`, stalled); accepting the stall in Edge cases (breaks the §8.10 60 s cadence and the maxTreeRecoverySeconds bound, and holds up watchdog enforcement for unrelated sessions); the return step on its own goroutine running alongside the release (contradicts SPEC-2 "It precedes the pod or slot release" and assumes an answer to open decision C); async only when `DelegationDepth > 0` (a conditional, and the seal step already stalls sweepers for every session with a local binding); a bounded worker pool (a new mechanism, and `cascadeToChildren` has no cap).
DECISION: the new rule goes in a new CODE-3 item 6 rather than item 1. BECAUSE item 1 is the home of the wait, and group G2 edits item 1 for the startup gate in the same round, so a separate item avoids a collision and keeps the caller rule in one place.
FACT: the hook is synchronous in every serial caller. `AfterTerminalWrite` calls `afterTerminalWrite` inline, and `OnSessionTerminal` calls `recordSessionCompleted` inline. Only `cascadeToChildren` and mcptools `cancelNode` already run it on a goroutine. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:77-83; usage.go:255-257, :776-792; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go:2360-2364
FACT: the tree already accepts a per-row seal stall in serial sweepers. The watchdog and orphancleanup stamp `TerminatedAt` inside each Update "because an earlier row's post-terminal hook, seal included, ran after the pass's now was read". The quiescence sleep counts from `TerminatedAt`, so it adds max(0, timeout − elapsed hook time) per delegated child, and the full timeout for a row with no local binding (resume_pending, pre-running, or bound on a peer). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:748-751; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:253-256
FACT: §12.8 **Lifecycle fence and drain** *Drain.* says "A successful drain does not wait for the session's terminal steps or the release of its pod", and erasure and tenant Phase 2 wait on the post-terminal window. An async `OnSessionTerminal` therefore does not break the drains. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:812; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner_phases.go and pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go reference the post-terminal window
FACT: terminal claims are refcounted per session (`terminalClaimRecord.open` increments, `end` decrements once), and `WaitTerminalWork` waits on them at shutdown. A claim the goroutine opens before the caller's own `end` runs therefore keeps the session claimed with no gap. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:96-113; sessionserver.go:1905-1930
WATCHOUT: do not move the goroutine into `AfterTerminalWrite` or `afterTerminalWrite`. `cancelSession` returns the hook's row (a seal relabel to failed), and `recordSessionCompleted` runs `afterTerminalRelease` on it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:473; usage.go:331-339
WATCHOUT: `failSession` reaches the hook through `afterFailed`, which `failFinalizing` and CODE-2's `failBudgetKeysExpired` share. Put the goroutine in `failSession`, not in `afterFailed`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:44-52, :94-96, :152-158
WATCHOUT: an async `OnSessionTerminal` lets a large watchdog or orphan pass run its seals concurrently. This is the `cascadeToChildren` precedent, and no spec bound limits it. Do not answer a later concurrency finding with a worker pool unless the finding names a spec bound that the fan-out breaks.
UNVERIFIED: these existing tests call `OnSessionTerminal`, `expireSession`, or `failSession` and may assert the hook's effects as soon as the call returns: tests/tier7a_load_local/{terminal_pipeline_context_race,shutdown_terminal_wait_race,stream_failure_report_race}_test.go, cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown_helper_test.go, and the sessionserver internal tests (terminal_writers, treerecovery, budget_forget, cancel_release, lifecycle, callback, elicitation_expiry, finalize_race, terminal_context). The implementor should check each one and add a `WaitTerminalWork` where needed.
OPEN: for the human deciding summary open decision C. An "alongside" variant of Option 2, with the return step on its own claimed goroutine counted from `TerminatedAt`, would also remove the writer-response and release delay that Edge cases bullet 6 accepts. CODE-3 item 6 is needed under either option, because the seal step already stalls the sweepers.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G2.1]

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: AfterTerminalWrite runs synchronously in its caller. Only cascadeToChildren (and CODE-2's cancelNodeGuarded) dispatch it on a goroutine, and that code says why: "so one descendant's seal window does not delay the next". The serial sweepers call the hook inline, row by row: orphancleanup.expireOrphan (§8.10 orphan sweep), orphansession.failOrphan, the watchdog's recordCompleted loops, treerecovery.RecoverTree's FailNode loop (via expireSession/failSession), and the §8.8 deadlock fail. Each one stamps TerminatedAt right before its hook, so CODE-3 item 1's sleep costs the full timeout for every delegated child in the loop. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:773-790; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:197-209,270; pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:227-243,394; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:736-771; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treerecovery/treerecovery.go:208-218; cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go:165
WATCHOUT: the serial-sweeper stall is not the pod or slot hold that open decision C owns. It comes from the sleep blocking the calling goroutine, so it exists under either option of C. Do not refute it as decision C.
FACT: the pre-existing loads are still not findings: one Grants PK read per delegate_task, a ListByRoot only when the sentinel is absent, a synchronous Postgres write per denial, and no etcd writes.
USEFUL [spec-recheck.1.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT]: it scoped the per-child pod hold to decision C, which kept the serial-loop finding separate from it.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]

FACT: the terminal context deadline and the CODE-3 sleep target use the same clock and the same anchor. `terminalContext` computes remaining = budget - (s.clock() - TerminatedAt) (sessionserver.go:2000-2006), and `writeTerminal` stamps TerminatedAt from s.clock() on the writer's replica, which is the replica that runs the hook (session_terminal.go:70-83). With q < seal enforced, the sleep always ends before the deadline unless the clock jumps. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2000, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:73

FACT: a cut CODE-2 descendant sweep (for example, on a cancelled delegate_task request ctx) has a reclaimer. orphancleanup expires every non-terminal node whose root has been terminal for longer than cascadeTimeoutSeconds (default 1 h). Do not file the request-ctx sweep as an unreclaimed leak. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:2-12
FACT: the decision C citations check out. `emitFinalUsage` has exactly two callers (pkg/adapter/session.go:477, pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:326), and the gateway has no FINAL_USAGE_REPORT consumer: its only mentions are comments at cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage.go:113-116.
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens for the recheck delta (SPEC-2 pool-admission clause, SPEC-3 detection wire code, the Clock reference edit, the checklist S18 dependency, and the decision C rewrite). BECAUSE: the return is single-shot per applied terminal write, a lost return fails closed and Edge cases bullet 1 accepts it, a hung SubtreeTokens read is bounded by the terminal ctx and accepted by the same bullet, and the drain wait in §10.1.7 covers the sleep. ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the parent observing child completion (archiveSettledChild in signals) up to q before the parallelChildren slot frees. Rejected because the pre-existing §8.3 quiescence step already waits before budget_return, so this is not a defect.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-security.1]
FACT: SubtreeTokens inputs are trusted in proxy mode: session_usage is written by the gateway LLM proxy recorder per request (cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:410-412). In direct mode the same store is fed from the adapter's ReportUsage pull (cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage.go directUsageLoop), which is a pod self-report, but spec §11.2 accepts that residual because direct mode is restricted to single-tenant or development deployments (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:55). Do not file the CODE-3 token credit as a self-report trust-boundary finding. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:55
FACT: the CODE-3 return fails closed on every error path. A Return error, a SubtreeTokens error (TokenDelta = 0), and a context that ends during the sleep all leave the reservation counted, so the budget becomes stricter and never looser. The startup quiescence gate refuses to start rather than degrading. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:48,53-54
WATCHOUT: sessionstore pgstore ListByRoot reads the read replica (pgstore.go:868-869, "task-tree projection routes to the read replica"), and CODE-4 item 1 uses it as the first-reservation witness. A lagging replica can only under-show delegated rows, so it biases the witness toward CreateTree. In the concurrent-first case that is harmless, because SET NX does not overwrite A's keys. After Redis key loss on a tree whose delegated rows are all younger than the replica lag, it would recreate zeroed counters. That needs a Redis disaster plus replica lag, so I judged it below the bar and did not file it. If a later lens reopens the witness, the remedy is a primary read in CODE-4 item 1, with no spec edit. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:860-869
FACT: the Decision C citations in the delta check out. pkg/adapter/session.go:477 and pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:326 are both emitFinalUsage calls. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:474-477

### [orchestrator.owner-decision-C]
DECISION: open decision C decided by the owner on 2026-10-28 and recorded as summary D3, superseding the owner-delegated order that placed the return before the release. The return's quiescence wait starts at the session's terminal transition and runs concurrently with the release; the return fires on the child adapter's `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` or its stream closing (§8.3 step 1, unchanged) or when `delegation.usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds` elapses from the terminal transition (step 2), whichever is first; a hung `Shutdown` cannot delay it past the timeout; decision B keeps it inside the §7.1 terminal pipeline budget; it runs from the terminal pipeline, on the recorded root, once; a return cut by process exit is not reclaimed or retried.
DECISION: rejected keeping the return before the release with the timeout as the only bound (final tokens uncredited; §8.3 step 1, §4.7.3, §28.5.2, §28.8, and §29.4 would need rewriting) and returning after the release completes (a hung `Shutdown` could consume the budget first).
DECISION: applied. Summary: D3 rewritten, decision C removed from Open decisions and recorded as decided, What changes, the release-order Watch-out, Non-goals (rejected orders; no §4.7.3/§28.5.2/§28.8/§29.4 edit), the usage-lag defect reason, and the SPEC-2 index line. Spec changes: SPEC-1's list places the return with the release, running concurrently; SPEC-2's ordering sentence states the concurrent rule and the whichever-first end; one spec Edge cases bullet on the timeout firing before the report. Non-spec: CODE-3 Targets, item 1 (waiter registered at hook entry for an admitted row, return on a goroutine, release not waiting for it, join after the release and forget), item 6 wording, new item 7 (`adapterclient.Client.AdapterEvents` reader and per-session latch), Testing item 3 cases (report during `Shutdown`, hung `Shutdown` firing at the timeout, stream close, process exit cutting the wait), Edge cases, and Files touched.
FACT: no gateway code opens `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` today; the proto client `AdapterEvents` exists, and the adapter refuses a second open with `FailedPrecondition` — EVIDENCE: grep over pkg/gateway and cmd/lenny-gateway (only a comment at adapterclient/client.go:571); pkg/proto/adapter/v1/lenny-adapter_grpc.pb.go:280; pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go `AdapterEvents`.
WATCHOUT: the cut-return Trap still holds; no "not retried" sentence went into SPEC-2. The implementation checklist was not in this edit's scope; its CODE-3 step may need item 7's new files and tiers.

SPEC-2's item-3 append (now Edit (a)) keeps ownership, recorded root, and the seal-window refusal only — BECAUSE the steps directly follow item 3 and a second statement there had already drifted from step 1 — ALTERNATIVES: keep the rule in the append and edit step 1 to agree (two homes); a one-sentence pointer in the append (adds nothing, step 1 follows immediately).
DECISION: the order is stated as two "does not wait" constraints — BECAUSE "runs concurrently with the release" and "does not precede the release" are false when the seal outlasts the timeout (the whole return finishes before the release begins; spec Edge cases bullet 1) — ALTERNATIVES: "runs concurrently with the seal and the release" (still false: also overlaps inbox drain and signals, and can end before the release).

### [spec.1.review-client-surface.1]

FACT: The AdapterEvents envelope is opaque bytes; the proto names spec §4.7.3's adapter-to-gateway table as the event taxonomy, so §4.7.3 is the wire-contract home for FINAL_USAGE_REPORT's fields and timing. The §4.7.3 row names no fields and says "just before the stream closes" — EVIDENCE: schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1881-1892; spec/04_system-components.md:788
FACT: The adapter sends FINAL_USAGE_REPORT once per session, from Shutdown's teardown, carrying sessionId, while the AdapterEvents stream is pod-wide and persists across sessions on reusable pods. §4.7 `Shutdown` row says the same ("It flushes the session's final usage report"). So "last message before the stream closes" (§4.7.3, §8.3 step 1, §28.5.2 Messages) is false on recycling and concurrent pods — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:474-477; pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go:80-99; spec/04_system-components.md:688
WATCHOUT: CODE-3 item 7 closes the replica's AdapterEvents stream after its last binding's release. The adapter treats a stream close while any started session remains as coordinator loss and enters hold state. On a concurrent pod whose other session's replica was refused a second stream (FailedPrecondition), that close puts a live session into hold — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go:92-108; pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:89-99
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-3 item 7 says the reader "closes it after the last such binding's release". That is false-safe on a pod holding another replica's live session: the adapter's onCoordinatorChannelClosed arms hold state for any started session. The non-spec loop needs to keep the stream open while the adapter holds any started session, or explain why a close cannot happen while another session is live.
FACT: Lease-extension dismissal reason is a free string on interactionstore.Interaction.Reason. The OpenAPI file has no enum for it, so LEASE_EXTENSION_REQUESTER_TERMINAL adds no client enum — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/interactionstore/interactionstore.go:61-62
USEFUL [Interaction dismiss reasons not client-visible]: re-verified; saved an OpenAPI/SDK sweep.
FACT: The spec has no enumeration of delegate_task error codes, so SPEC-3 Edit (b)'s INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION needs no spec table row. The docs table at docs/runtime-author-guide/platform-tools.md is a non-spec site.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]

FACT: Every new tree's first delegate_task under SPEC-3 Edit (b) costs one extra budget_reserve.lua run that exits at the sentinel EXISTS check, plus one SessionStore read of the root's rows (only the root row for a new tree). Against the §8.3 Tier 3 Lua contention budget (100 µs per script, 50 concurrent scripts reach the 5 ms LeaseStore SLO), an early-exit run is negligible and is not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:380-395 (**Aggregate contention formula.**)
FACT: SPEC-2's concurrent quiescence wait holds one timer per terminating delegated child for at most usageQuiescenceTimeoutSeconds (default 5 s). At Tier 3 delegation rates of 500 to 1,000/s that is at most about 5,000 waiters across the whole cluster, all held in memory with no store writes. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:390 ("sustained Tier 3 delegation rates of 500–1,000/s"), :426
FACT: Redis reset is covered by §12.4: delegate_task is rejected during reconciliation, so a reset that has been reconciled does not trigger SPEC-3's detection path. The reconciler code gap is already the summary defect **Redis recovery misses trees**. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:228, :233
FACT: The new Postgres dependencies (the SessionStore and row reads in SPEC-3 (b) and SPEC-4 (c), and the denial write before REJECTED in SPEC-4 (d)) fail closed during a Postgres failover. delegate_task already depended on Postgres through the child-row insert, and grants already did through the row transaction, so this degrades no further than the shipped design.
DECISION: no findings under the performance and reliability lens — BECAUSE no staged spec edit adds etcd writes, a hot key, or a net-new watch, and every failure path fails closed and degrades no further than the shipped design — ALTERNATIVES: filing the extra first-reservation Lua run (rejected because it is negligible against the stated budget).

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one contradiction: SPEC-2 says the quiescence wait "starts at the child's terminal transition" and also "does not precede the release"; the seal sits between those two points (§7.1 Seal-and-export invariant), and spec Edge cases bullet 1 has the return run before the release when the seal outlasts the timeout — BECAUSE an implementor can read "does not precede" as gating the wait on the release's start, which moves the timeout's origin off the terminal transition that decision B's bound depends on — ALTERNATIVES: filing the step 1 trigger ("On receiving a terminal lifecycle event") vs SPEC-2's start point, rejected because the owner's decision C records "§8.3 step 1 is unchanged".
FACT: the adapter emits FINAL_USAGE_REPORT per session inside Shutdown's reclaimed-slot teardown, so a slot release on a multi-session pod also reaches step 1 — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:474-477
FACT: Redis-outage returns: §12.4 fails delegate_task closed until the §11.2 reconstruction, which recounts live descendants, so a reservation never sees keys lost by a failover before reconstruction; the first-reservation witness is not exposed to failover key loss under the spec — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:228, :233
UNVERIFIED: none of the other staged edits (SPEC-3..SPEC-9) showed a reliability defect; the SPEC-5 dismissal works across replicas because CODE-7 polls the SessionStore on each tick (non-spec CODE-7 item 1).

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
- FACT: FINAL_USAGE_REPORT (a pod/adapter message) only ends the quiescence wait; the credited amount comes from gateway-side counters (§8.3 step 2 "last known usage counter"; proxy mode ignores pod ReportUsage per §11.2 and §4.9 "Authoritative token counting"). An early or forged report can only shorten the wait, which matches the accepted edge case (late tokens credited back), so it does not move a trust boundary. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:54, spec/04_system-components.md:1968, spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:425-427
- FACT: every security bound SPEC-4 touches is Postgres-durable and fails closed: grant pool cap and denial re-check run in the row-locked transaction, and Edit (d) fails the request with no grant when the denial write fails. Per-replica consent state (open elicitation, batch, success cool-off) loses only auto-grant convenience when lost. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md §8.6 step 3 cool-off and step 4 sub-bullets
- FACT: the spec states no GC of terminal session rows (I ran a meaning search over §7, §11, and §12 that judged every passage), so the SPEC-3 (b) first-reservation witness cannot be fooled by purged child rows while the root lives.
- UNVERIFIED: the witness read uses `ListByRoot`, which reads the read replica (Standing context "Read replicas"). A child row committed within the replica-lag window while the tree's keys are truly lost could be missed, and the marked rerun would then recreate the keys at full budget. This needs a coincident key loss, so I did not file it. A code-loop reviewer should check whether the witness read must go to the primary.
- USEFUL [Standing context: Lease-extension trigger is in-process; Interaction dismiss reasons not client-visible]: these two entries closed the tenant-pinning and client-surface questions without any re-investigation.

True instead: the adapter pushes FINAL_USAGE_REPORT for one session, during that session's Shutdown, after in-flight ReportUsage pulls settle. The comments at pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go:35-38 and pkg/adapter/adapterevents_test.go:182 have the same stale wording.
FACT: The SPEC-2 Edit (a) bound refuses a pool when its seal window is not greater than the timeout. That is enough for "the wait ends before the release allowance begins", because §7.1 counts the budget from the terminal transition as the largest pool seal window plus the release allowance (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116). "Pool admission" means the gateway pool admission path, which can see gateway settings, as spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:678 and spec/04_system-components.md:1325 show. It is not the in-process webhook. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:678
WATCHOUT: The adapter refuses a second CH-ADAPTEREVENTS stream with FailedPrecondition, and it treats a closed stream as coordinator loss (it enters hold). §28.5.2 Exclusivity addresses events to the coordinating replica. So a terminal pipeline on a replica that does not coordinate the pod times out, and that is the correct degraded path under SPEC-2 rather than a spec defect. Whether a gateway-initiated close after release puts a still-live co-tenant session's adapter into hold is a code question for the non-spec loop. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/adapterevents.go:90-106; spec/28_communication-channels.md:501-508

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
- FACT: The staged spec edits add no etcd write, no new watch, and no new Redis key. Load the edits do add: one goroutine for each delegated child's terminal hook, held until FINAL_USAGE_REPORT arrives, the stream closes, or the timeout (5 s default) passes, plus the shipped one SubtreeTokens read and one budget_return.lua call. That load is constant per terminal and is not a single-key hotspot. The Redis counters stay per root under the shared `{root_session_id}` hash tag, the same as in the shipped design. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 Edit (b); spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116.
- FACT: §28.5.2 **Exclusivity.** says the spec does not state which replica's connection carries an event when more than one replica holds a connection to a pod. When the replica running the hook is not the replica receiving the stream, the wait therefore falls back to the timeout. That fallback is no worse than the shipped design, which never consumes the report. It is code-side (CODE-3 item 7) and is not a spec-loop finding. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:501-507.
- FACT: The bound in SPEC-2 Edit (a) requires each pool's maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds to be greater than the timeout. That is stricter than the §7.1 budget needs, because the budget uses the largest seal window of any pool. The stricter condition is a design choice and not a defect. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116; spec-changes.md:41.
- FACT: §10.1 **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** already waits for in-flight pipelines for up to the budget. The joined return adds at most the timeout, and that addition stays inside the seal window. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:179.
- USEFUL [Perf standing entry]: confirmed that the Grants PK read and the row-lock serialization need no re-check.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: delete CODE-3 item 7 (the gateway `adapterclient.Client.AdapterEvents` client, the per-pod reader, its open in bind_registration.go/resume_driver.go, and the session-keyed latch in usage_quiescence.go). CODE-3's wait becomes timer-only: from `TerminatedAt` to the usage quiescence timeout, still concurrent with the release (decision C order unchanged). The reason lives in one place, a new summary defect **The gateway has no `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` consumer**. D3, summary line 8, and CODE-3 item 1 point to it — BECAUSE the adapter arms its pod-wide §10.1.4 hold on ANY end of the stream, a clean io.EOF included, whenever any session on the pod is started. The hold timeout then terminates every started session on the pod, so any gateway client that lands before R12's corrected entry condition activates a hold path that is dead code in production today. The remediation plan already assigns the client, the one-owner-per-pod rule, FailedPrecondition arbitration, and "FINAL_USAGE_REPORT feeds the budget return" to R12, and it states that "the client and a correct entry condition land together". The report's totals are not applied (the return reads SubtreeTokens), so the reader only shortened the wait by up to 5 s. — ALTERNATIVES: (1) close only when the pod leaves the registry or retires: a gateway restart still arms the hold on every such pod, which is R12's named hazard. (2) Stage an adapter change so a clean io.EOF arms no hold: this is R12's entry-condition work (L-sized, "highest behavior-change surface"), and a replica crash still holds sessions coordinated by other replicas. (3) Open the stream only on maxConcurrentSessions 1 pools: a tier-dependent path, and the handoff/FailedPrecondition corners remain. (4) Keep the latch as a seam for R12: R12 owns the per-event fan-out design, and a firer with no production caller is speculative.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: The adapter arms the §10.1.4 coordinator hold whenever its CH-ADAPTEREVENTS stream ends with any started session on the pod, a clean gateway-side close (io.EOF) included.
