### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]
FACT: The hook's terminal context cannot be cancelled by a graceful shutdown. `terminalContext` returns `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), remaining)`, `Server.Close` cancels only `lifetimeCancel`, and cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go `run` calls `waitTerminalWork()` before `sessions.Close()`. Only the deadline ends the wait (budget exhaustion, or SIGKILL/crash ending the process). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2005, :1895-1897; cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:66-67
WATCHOUT: Do not write a test case that has "a `Server` shutdown" or "the test cancels" end the terminal context during the wait. A graceful shutdown drains the joined return and issues the `Return` (which the CODE-3 item 6 `WaitTerminalWork` bullet already asserts). A test ends the wait only through the context's deadline: a short terminal pipeline budget, or a past `TerminatedAt` with the seam held. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Testing item 3 bullet "a terminal context that ends during the wait"
MISTAKE: Round 2 deleted the report-inside-Shutdown case together with CODE-3 item 7 and left only "Return issued while Shutdown is still blocked", which a wait-then-release implementation also passes. The discriminating observation has to be taken while the seam is still unfired: the executor release (`releaseExecutor` in `releaseAfterTerminal`) has already started at that point.
DECISION: Process-exit bullet deleted, and its no-reclaim clause folded into the cut-wait bullet. BECAUSE its premise was false and that clause was the only part the preceding bullet lacked. ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "the test cancels, standing in for SIGKILL" (a WithoutCancel-derived context cannot be cancelled, and it would leave a duplicate bullet); a positive graceful-shutdown bullet (the item 6 bullet already covers that case).
DECISION: The blocked-release bullet now leads with "with the wait held on its timer seam, the executor release starts" and drops "with no report sent". BECAUSE CODE-3 item 1 observes no step 1 signal, and the tier-1 seam is the executor rather than the adapter. ALTERNATIVES: appending to the old bullet (longer, and it keeps the non-discriminating lead); restoring the FINAL_USAGE_REPORT case (the staged mechanism has no consumer).

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: no shutdown path ends the hook's terminal context. `terminalContext` is `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), remaining)` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go terminalContext); `Close` cancels only `lifetimeCancel`; cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go `run` calls `waitTerminalWork` (bounded by `TerminalPipelineBudget()`) before `Close`. So a graceful shutdown during the return's wait drains it and the `Return` IS issued; only SIGKILL, a crash, or the budget cuts it. — EVIDENCE: sessionserver.go terminalContext/Close/WaitTerminalWork, shutdown.go run/waitTerminalWork
DECISION: fold the process-exit bullet's no-reclaim half into the preceding "terminal context that ends during the wait" bullet and delete the process-exit bullet — BECAUSE both bullets asserted the same no-`Return` behaviour and the only new content was "a new `Server` issues none for that child"; the graceful-drain positive case is already the `WaitTerminalWork` bullet (CODE-3 item 6) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "a terminal context that the test cancels, standing in for SIGKILL" (rejected: a test cannot cancel a WithoutCancel-derived context, SIGKILL ends the process rather than the context, and it keeps two bullets asserting one rule).
WATCHOUT: a tier-1 test ends the terminal context only through its deadline (a short terminal pipeline budget, or a `TerminatedAt` in the past, with the timer seam held); the wait must select on `ctx.Done()` beside the seam. Do not add a lifetime-linked cancel to the terminal context to make a shutdown test pass: it contradicts §10.1.7's drain. — EVIDENCE: sessionserver.go terminalContext
DECISION: rewrite the "release whose adapter `Shutdown` blocks" bullet so the discriminating assertion is that the release starts while the wait is still held on its timer seam — BECAUSE wait-then-release passes every other bullet (it issues the `Return` when the seam fires, then blocks in the release); "with no report sent" is dropped because CODE-3 item 1 observes no step 1 signal, so a report changes nothing — ALTERNATIVES: appending the reviewer's clause to the existing bullet (leaves the non-discriminating "while `Shutdown` is still blocked" as the lead assertion and the vestigial report clause).
FACT: at tier 1 the release is observable as the fake executor's release call (`releaseExecutor(ctx, s.executor, ...)` in terminal_hook.go `releaseAfterTerminal`), not an adapter `Shutdown`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go releaseAfterTerminal

### [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 5 — BECAUSE the r3->r5 diff only rewrites two Testing item 3 bullets and adds no store write, watch, key, or goroutine. CODE-3 item 7 (the AdapterEvents reader) is gone, so the wait always runs the full usage quiescence timeout (summary defect **The gateway has no `CH-ADAPTEREVENTS` consumer**). The release does not wait for it, so the only cost is a parent `parallelChildren` slot held up to 5 s longer, which owner decision D3 accepts — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unbounded hook goroutines from CODE-3 item 6 (rejected: non-spec.3 DECISION at review-log:736/757 weighed a bounded pool, and cascadeToChildren is uncapped today); filing SubtreeTokens O(tree) reads per child (rejected: settled at review-log:391/1288).
FACT: the terminal context can never end during the quiescence wait in production. The budget is seal window + release allowance (sessionserver.go:1969-1973), the startup gate keeps the timeout below the seal window, and both the wait and terminalContext count from TerminatedAt on s.clock(). So the Testing item 3 "terminal context that ends during the wait" case needs a test Server built with a seal window below the timeout (sessionserver Options do not run the cmd gate), or a TerminatedAt ahead of the clock — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1969-2006
FACT: the shutdown wait on terminal claims is bounded by the terminal pipeline budget and precedes Server.Close, so the extra ≤5 s hook tail fits inside it — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:63-110

FACT: Shutdown waits for the terminal claim record for at most TerminalPipelineBudget (seal + release allowance) and Close does not cancel the terminal context; the return at TerminatedAt+Q (Q < seal) fits inside that wait — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:93-97; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1918
FACT: ReportAttachStreamFailure runs the failure funnel (and so the hook with its join) synchronously on the CH-ATTACH reader goroutine, bounded by seal + report timeout; the join adds at most Q inside that existing bound — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/stream_failure.go:45-70
FACT: terminal claims are in-memory per replica (terminalClaimRecord); startRelease defers a terminal binding release while a claim is open, so the held return only delays the coordination Sweeper's release by up to Q — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:88-125

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]

- FACT: The consumption that CODE-3's token credit reads comes from a trusted source. `sessionusage` is written only by the gateway recorder (`cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:410-412`). Direct-mode deltas reach that recorder through the gateway's own `ReportUsage` pull (`cmd/lenny-gateway/direct_usage.go:288`, `RecordDirectUsage`), which §8.6 and §8.10 accept as the direct-mode measure. CODE-3 does not apply `FINAL_USAGE_REPORT` totals, so no pod self-report sets the credit. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:412; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:633
- FACT: The tenant for the lease extension is gateway-derived. `sessionbudget.ExtendOnExhaustion` already carries `tenantID` (`pkg/gateway/session/sessionbudget/sessionbudget.go:102`), and `leaseExtendSeam` currently drops it (`cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:297`). CODE-6's "passes the tenant it receives" passes that in-process value through. EVIDENCE: controlserver.go:296-298
- FACT: Hooks moved onto goroutines by CODE-3 item 6 stay covered at process exit. `WaitTerminalWork` waits on `terminalClaims` (`pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1918`), and item 6 keeps the claim open until the hook ends. EVIDENCE: sessionserver.go:1904-1928
- DECISION: I filed no security finding in round 5. BECAUSE the fail-closed paths hold: a `SubtreeTokens` error credits zero, a cut return leaks the reservation, a quiescence timeout of 0 resolves to 5 s and the startup gate still refuses it, and the grant clamp runs under the row lock. ALTERNATIVES: I considered and rejected the `ListByRoot` replica-lag counter reset after Redis key loss, which is already a standing Open and is unreachable while a Redis restart outlasts replica lag. I also rejected the delay of `expireRaisedElicitations` by the return join, which runs after the release and so is no regression of §9.2.

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: CODE-3 item 7 (gateway CH-ADAPTEREVENTS client) is gone from the current text; CODE-3 item 1 waits to TerminatedAt + timeout unconditionally, and the consumer is the summary defect deferred to R12. Directive text about item 7 is stale. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:48, summary.md:103
FACT: every OnSessionTerminal caller (watchdog recordCompleted, orphancleanup, orphansession, deadlock fail, budget terminator, admin and erasure adapters) calls it inline per row in a loop; CODE-3 item 6 makes all of them asynchronous, for non-delegated rows too. cascadeToChildren and mcptools cancelNode already run hooks on unbounded detached goroutines, so this follows the existing pattern. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1587-1590, cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:94-98, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools.go:2360-2364
FACT: archiveSettledChild already runs TreeUsage (ListByRoot + treearchive Replay) for each settled child, so SubtreeTokens adds a second O(tree) read on the read replica per child terminal; maxTreeSize defaults to 50, so the cost is small. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:927-929, resultrollup.go:135-141, spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:125
DECISION: no findings under the performance lens. BECAUSE the per-child cost is one goroutine plus one terminal claim held for <= the timeout, the added reads are O(tree) at the default tree size, CODE-8 sweep cost is 2 queries per 500 ids per 60 s per replica, and the Redis key-loss failure mode is settled as D6 plus the defect "Redis recovery misses trees". ALTERNATIVES: considered and dropped (1) the unbounded concurrent seal fan-out from item 6 at Tier 3, because no stated budget is shown to be breached and the pattern already ships in cascadeToChildren; (2) the slot turnover delay of about 5 s per child (BUDGET_EXHAUSTED on immediate re-delegation), because it follows directly from D3 plus the R12 defect and the spec-level wait already allowed it; (3) the 250 ms elicit poll gaining a SessionStore.Get, because it doubles an existing per-tree poll and no budget is named.
OPEN: the Testing item 3 tier-4 "race and cancel losers, then delegate maxParallelChildren more times" test must re-delegate after TerminatedAt + the quiescence timeout, because slots return only then (no consumer before R12). An implementor who re-delegates immediately sees BUDGET_EXHAUSTED.

### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: the shipped child return credits zero tokens (`returnTreeBudget` sends only TreeMemoryDelta and ParallelChildrenDelta), so CODE-3's `max(0, slice − SubtreeTokens)` is a new credit. `session_usage` is fed by both the proxy path (gateway-measured) and `RecordDirectUsage` (direct-mode adapter ReportUsage pulls) through `recordAccounting`. In direct mode the credit therefore rests on pod-reported usage, but §8.3 Delegation Lease item 5 **Over-run semantics** already says the return uses actual consumption "regardless of delivery mode" and accepts direct-mode over-run, so this is existing spec posture and not a regression the proposal introduces. DECISION: security lens returned no findings in round 7 — BECAUSE tenant pinning (CODE-6 items 2, 5, 6), fail-closed reads (CODE-4 Grants and ListByRoot), Postgres-authoritative grants and denials, and audit surfacing (CODE-2 metric, event, and alert) all hold, and the round-6 diff only adds test files and test cases — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the witness-reset risk (first-reservation re-creating counters after child rows vanish), but summary D5 records that only erasure deletes session rows, so the risk is speculative

### [non-spec.9.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: CODE-2 item 2 hands the root write's terminal claim to a detached goroutine that runs `afterFailed`, pointing at CODE-3 item 6's `failSession` pattern, and goes on to item 3 without waiting — BECAUSE `afterFailed` runs `AfterTerminalWrite` inline (seal up to the seal window, then release), which held the step 3(b) sweep and the detecting `delegate_task` answer behind the root's whole pipeline, against §8.3's "immediate tree cleanup" — ALTERNATIVES: adding `failBudgetKeysExpired` to CODE-3 item 6's writer list (two edits and a rewrite of item 6's stated loop reason); sweeping before the root write (the applied root write picks the single event emitter); accepting the delay as an Edge case (contradicts SPEC-3 Edit (b)).
FACT: `afterFailed` is `s.AfterTerminalWrite(ctx, fromState, updated, s.settledChildSignals)`, and the hook body runs `sealWorkspace` then `releaseAfterTerminal` inline; `cascadeToChildren` already runs each node's hook on a detached goroutine — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:94-95, terminal_hook.go:99-116, usage.go:778.
FACT: §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** states that the end of the caller that made the terminal write does not end the pipeline, so detaching the root's hook needs no spec edit — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116.
WATCHOUT: the root's terminal claim must stay held until the goroutine's hook ends, or §10.1.7 process-exit waits and `WaitTerminalWork` stop covering the root's seal; Testing item 2's held-seal case is the discriminating test, because Testing item 4's tier-4 end states hold whether the hook is inline or detached — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1918 (`WaitTerminalWork` polls `terminalClaims.size()`).
DECISION: Testing item 3 gains a blocking-sealer case asserting the `Return` is issued at `TerminatedAt` plus the timeout while the seal is still blocked — BECAUSE every listed timing case used an instant seal, so an implementation starting the return goroutine after `sealWorkspace` passed them all — ALTERNATIVES: folding it into the timer-seam/release case (a compound two-run case); adding "the release runs after the seal returns" (the hook order already pins it and it does not discriminate).

### [non-spec.9.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: F-11.2.31's crash-window residue (a budget termination a crash cuts before its terminal write is not retried; next replica's enforcer counts from zero) is declined and recorded as a summary defect entry "**A budget termination lost to a crash is not retried.**"; the Non-goal "Seeding the per-session enforcer from durable `sessionusage`." only points at it — BECAUSE §11.2 enforces per-session budget against Redis counters with **Crash Recovery for Quota Counters:**, so the right fix is moving `sessionbudget.Enforcer` onto shared counters, which is §11.2 work outside the P4b seam — ALTERNATIVES: seeding the enforcer from `sessionusage` on first Record (lagging best-effort replica read, a second count source, papers over the §11.2 deviation); moving enforcement onto Redis inside 0107 (correct but out of scope); narrowing the F-11.2.31 Goal (the Goal claims reclamation, which CODE-5 and CODE-8 deliver).
WATCHOUT: do not add a CODE item or test that seeds the enforcer from `sessionusage`; the residue is filed, not staged. After approval the defects entry must be filed into BUILD-GAPS (F-11.2.31 itself closes against CODE-5/CODE-8, so the entry is the only open record) — EVIDENCE: BUILD-GAPS.md:18443 (0100 resolution names the residue), :18451 (pointer note), gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md (0100 defects routed to P4 under F-11.2.31).
FACT: `sessionbudget.Enforcer` keeps `sessions map[string]*counter` in process memory, and its doc comment claims the in-memory path is the §11.2 fail-open path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionbudget/sessionbudget.go:105-117; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:57 ("The gateway enforces budget limits against Redis counters (fast path)") and :59.

### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: `failBudgetKeysExpired` hands its root claim to a goroutine that runs `afterFailed` on a detached context (pointer to CODE-3 item 6's `failSession` pattern), replacing the inline call in CODE-2 item 2 — BECAUSE the inline `AfterTerminalWrite` runs the root's seal (up to the seal window) and release before step 3(b)'s sweep and before `delegate_task` answers — ALTERNATIVES: adding `failBudgetKeysExpired` to CODE-3 item 6's writer list (two edits and rewrites item 6's loop-of-rows reason); running the sweep before the root write (breaks the "only the applied writer emits" winner decision in CODE-2 item 3).
FACT: `afterFailed` is a synchronous wrapper over `AfterTerminalWrite`, whose body runs `sealWorkspace` then `releaseAfterTerminal` inline; `cascadeToChildren` already detaches each descendant hook with `context.WithoutCancel` and a handed-off `BeginTerminal` claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:94-95, terminal_hook.go:100-116, usage.go:772-790
WATCHOUT: the root is not a delegated child, so its hook starts no return goroutine; the delay the fix removes is seal plus release, not the quiescence wait — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 1 ("A row that item 2 does not admit ... starts no goroutine")
WATCHOUT: Testing item 3's "`failBudgetKeysExpired` on the root issues no `Return` and exactly one forget" stays true after the detach, but the test has to wait on `WaitTerminalWork` before it asserts the forget, because the claim is handed to the goroutine.
DECISION: the seal-before-return ordering test is a new Testing item 3 bullet with a blocking sealer, rather than a rewrite of the existing timer-seam/release bullet — BECAUSE merging both blocking steps into one "or" clause reads as either-run-suffices.

### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: F-11.2.31's crash-window residue (a budget termination lost to a crash before its terminal write is not retried) is recorded as declined: one new entry in summary "Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage", and the bare Non-goal "Seeding the per-session enforcer from durable `sessionusage`." gains a pointer to it — BECAUSE §11.2 already says the gateway enforces budget limits against Redis counters and restores them after a crash (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.2 "The gateway enforces budget limits against Redis counters (fast path)" and **Crash Recovery for Quota Counters:**), so the real fix is moving `sessionbudget.Enforcer` onto shared counters, which is §11.2 enforcement work outside the P4b tree-budget seam — ALTERNATIVES: seeding the enforcer from `sessionusage` on first Record rejected (a second, lagging count source: `recordAccounting` writes it best-effort and `Get` reads the replica pool; it is a partial patch over a spec deviation rather than the §11.2 mechanism); keying on a session generation is already rejected as D12.
FACT: the shipped per-session enforcer is process-memory only and creates an entry at zero on first Record, so a session recovered on another replica after a crash can spend another full budget. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionbudget/sessionbudget.go:216 `Record`, :365 `Forget`; BUILD-GAPS.md:18451 pointer note "This finding owns that crash window."; proposals/0100_*/0100_*summary.md:113.
WATCHOUT: after approval the new defects entry must be filed into BUILD-GAPS (file-unstaged-defects), because F-11.2.31's pointer note names it as the home of the crash window; closing F-11.2.31 against CODE-5/CODE-8 without refiling loses the only open record.

### [non-spec.9.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: The terminal context can end during the return's quiescence wait only in a Server built with a seal window at or below the timeout (tier-1 Options), because terminalContext bounds by seal + stream-failure-report timeout from TerminatedAt, the wait ends at TerminatedAt + timeout, and the startup gate holds timeout < seal. Process exit does not end it (WithoutCancel). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2000-2006
- FACT: A CODE-2 sweep cut by the delegate_task request context (HandleBudgetKeysExpired runs on the tool-call ctx, and cascadeToChildren's writeTerminal uses the ctx it receives) has two reclaimers: CODE-2 step 4 re-sweeps on the next detection, and orphancleanup expires any node whose root has been terminal past cascadeTimeoutSeconds (default 3600 s). So it is slower recovery, not an orphan with no reclaimer. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:1-12, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:782
- DECISION: Returned no findings — BECAUSE the Return's lack of a retry is the fail-closed choice (DECRBY is not idempotent, so a retry after an ambiguous error could double-credit), no spec text requires a return to be replayed after a Redis outage (the meaning search over §8, §11, and §12 found none), and Redis failover that loses a sentinel is covered by the filed defect **Redis recovery misses trees** — ALTERNATIVES: filing the request-context cleanup cut (rejected, a bounded reclaimer exists) and the lost-grant-on-crash headroom consumption (rejected, subsumed by F-11.2.31's process-local enforcer).
- FACT: No chart, compose, or test config sets the seal window below 6 s except tests/testinfra/kind/e2e-values.yaml:177 (which CODE-3 raises). The chart renders the flag only when the value is > 0 (default 0 → 300 s). — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:475-476

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
- FACT: the staged spec edits create no etcd or CRD writes. The Postgres cost they add is one `ListByRoot` read and one extra `budget_reserve.lua` run per tree, at the tree's first reservation and on a sentinel-absent rerun. They add three `SessionStore` reads per extension request, and one synchronous denial write per `REJECTED`. None of these is a bottleneck at the top tier — EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-3 Edit (b), SPEC-4 Edit (c), and Edit (d)
- FACT: on Redis recovery, §11.2 and §12.4 rebuild every active tree's counters, the `tokens` sentinel included, before any reservation is admitted. The first-reservation witness therefore applies only to trees that the reconstruction missed, and the shipped design already fails those trees. Grants now live in the `delegation_tree_budget` row's `ext_*` columns, so a Redis reset does not lose them — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:61, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:233
- FACT: §7.1 counts the terminal pipeline budget from the terminal transition, as the largest seal window of any pool plus the release allowance. A per-pool seal window that exceeds the quiescence timeout therefore suffices for the SPEC-2 Edit (a) claim — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116
- DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE every Postgres-failover path fails closed and retryable on a path that already needed the primary for the child insert, the handoff loss of consent state yields no grant, and a cut return is a settled Non-goal — ALTERNATIVES: filing the replica-lag witness Open was rejected, because a harmful miss needs key loss within the replication lag of a child commit, which is speculative
- USEFUL [Standing context: Perf; Redis recovery contract; Cut return accepted]: these entries removed the need to re-derive the grant row-lock cost and the reconstruction gap

### [spec.1.review-security.1]

- DECISION: no security findings on SPEC-1..SPEC-10 — BECAUSE every bound the staging touches has a trusted, durable authority: the grant pool clamps inside the delegation_tree_budget row transaction (Postgres), the denial is written before REJECTED, reservation detection and a failed SessionStore/row read both fail closed, and the return's token figure is gateway-recorded (SubtreeTokens) with FINAL_USAGE_REPORT used only as a timing signal — ALTERNATIVES: filing the FINAL_USAGE_REPORT `session_id` self-report (SPEC-10 (a)) as an attacker-influenceable bound; rejected, an early or forged report only shortens the 5 s wait, the same over-credit SPEC-2's edge case already accepts.
- FACT: Edit (d) skipping step 4 (event) for a non-failable or terminal root does not hide the degraded state: §16.5 `DelegationBudgetKeysExpired` fires on `lenny_delegation_budget_keys_expired_total`, which SPEC-8 (a) increments on every detection — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:84, :446
- FACT: no spec path or code deletes a delegated child's session row while its root lives (archiveSettledChild writes session_tree_archive and keeps the row), so the SPEC-3 first-reservation witness cannot be emptied to reset counters short of erasure/tenant deletion — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:555-585

The Lua return then runs in the release allowance, and the §10.1.7 exit wait covers it through SPEC-1 — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:116, spec/10_gateway-internals.md:179
- USEFUL [Open: ListByRoot replica lag on the witness]: lag cannot fail a new tree. A lagging witness only leads to a marked rerun, which creates the missing keys. A missed row with keys absent would need the keys to expire within replica lag of the first child's commit, and the TTL counts 8 days from key creation, which precedes every child insert.

### [spec.3.review-security.1]

- FACT: §8.6 success cool-off auto-grants with no elicitation (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:727). Making it replica-local (SPEC-4 Edit (c)) cannot produce a grant after a rejection, because the in-flight atomic check re-reads the row's denial inside the grant transaction (:736), and SPEC-4 keeps that check on the grant write. A replica that loses its state opens a new elicitation and grants nothing. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:727, :736
- FACT: the SPEC-2 Edit (a) pool-admission refusal is spec text the tree cannot implement yet, and the proposal does this on purpose: summary defect **Pool admission has no seal window to compare**, non-spec CODE-3 item 1, and DOC-1 item 3 all say the code ships only the startup check. Do not file it as a contradiction. EVIDENCE: summary.md:102, non-spec-changes.md:48, :170
- DECISION: no security findings this round. BECAUSE every bound the staged edits touch has a trusted, durable source. The grant pool is clamped inside the Postgres row transaction, and denials are written before `REJECTED` is returned. Row or SessionStore read failures fail closed with `DELEGATION_BUDGET_UNAVAILABLE`. Detection fails closed, and the request tenant is derived by the gateway. A return that finds the keys missing writes nothing. ALTERNATIVES: I considered ListByRoot replica lag as a budget-reset path, but it needs Redis key loss within seconds of the first child insert. It is already a standing Open, and the fix candidate is a non-spec primary read.

### [spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings for the performance and failure-mode lens on the round-5 spec staging — BECAUSE the r3->r5 diff is two wording changes in SPEC-4 Edit (a) and SPEC-5, and the staging adds no etcd write, no new watch, and no per-request Redis write. The new Postgres work is one SessionStore read per tree's first reservation (SPEC-3 Edit (b), single-shard because tree sessions share the routing prefix), a delegation_tree_budget read per reservation, and a synchronous denial write per REJECTED (SPEC-4 Edit (d)), all at delegate_task or extension rates. — ALTERNATIVES: filing per-replica elicitations as a scaling defect, rejected because SPEC-4 Edit (c) states the outcome and the Edge-cases list accepts it; filing concurrent reservation detections that each run a cascade, rejected because the burst is bounded by tree size and happens only on a critical-alert condition.
FACT: a Postgres failover makes delegate_task fail retryably under SPEC-3 Edit (b). That is no regression, because child-session creation already needs Postgres. A Redis reset is handled by §11.2/§12.4 reconstruction before delegate_task resumes. Returns no longer trigger cleanup, so key loss is less aggressive than in the shipped design. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:228, :233; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:61; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:136 (tree co-location)
WATCHOUT: CH-ADAPTEREVENTS addresses events to the coordinating replica (spec/28_communication-channels.md:1965-1968). A terminal pipeline that runs on another replica therefore never sees FINAL_USAGE_REPORT, and its return runs on the timeout. This is covered by the settled D3/R12 deferral ("the wait ends at the timeout until R12"), so do not file it as a new finding.
USEFUL [Cut return accepted]: saved re-deriving that a crash-cut return (§10.1 "Terminal pipelines at process exit.", spec/10_gateway-internals.md:179) is an accepted fail-closed loss.

### [non-spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
MISTAKE: CODE-3 item 6 **Hook dispatch** row for the `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` root write kept "detached | own, handed to the goroutine" after its hook route became `OnSessionTerminal`; now "detached, by that row | own, ended when `OnSessionTerminal` returns", per rule 2 and CODE-2 item 2. ### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` root write reaches the hook through `OnSessionTerminal` (recordSessionCompleted: terminalSignals + afterTerminalRelease), dispatched detached with its own claim handed to the goroutine — BECAUSE `afterFailed` = AfterTerminalWrite + settledChildSignals (archive + lifecycle audit only) and so skips the §14 callback, the §16.6 event, the session-log close, the quota checkpoint, and the §11.2.1 `session.completed` billing event — ALTERNATIVES: a root-only signal tail (a second terminal pipeline, and it would touch failSession/failFinalizing); an inline call with the claim "ended when OnSessionTerminal returns" (blocks the handler on the root seal at S11, before S15 makes OnSessionTerminal detached; S11→S15→S13→S11 is a cycle, so the steps cannot be reordered).
FACT: `enqueueTerminalCallback` is called only inside `terminalSignals`, and `billingstore.EventSessionCompleted` is emitted only in `afterTerminalRelease` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:331-460 (`recordSessionCompleted` :331, call :376, `afterTerminalRelease` :400, billing :448); session_terminal.go:94-102 (`afterFailed`, `settledChildSignals`).
WATCHOUT: the CODE-2 3(b) sweep stays even though the root's `terminalSignals` now runs `cascadeToChildren`. The cascade honours `detach` while §8.3 cleanup item 1 cancels every descendant, and the sweep is the only cancel when the guard refuses the root. The two race under `nonTerminalGuard`, and the loser gets ErrSuperseded. Do not delete the sweep or add a cascade skip — EVIDENCE: non-spec CODE-2 item 3(b); usage.go:729 cascadeToChildren.
DECISION: `budgetKeysExpiredGuard` stamps FailureClass and FailureReason, and `writeTerminal` writes `failed` — BECAUSE a writer whose own mutator assigns a literal terminal State is picked up by the inventory scan and needs `terminalWriterInventory` and `terminalContextWriters` rows; through writeTerminal it stays under writeTerminal's existing row — ALTERNATIVES: add inventory rows (it duplicates writeTerminal's mutator); move the writer into session_terminal.go (renames the file in CODE-2 and the CODE-3 table).
FACT: `TestTerminalWriterInventoryMatchesTheCode_spec_7_2` requires each `writeTerminalCallers` name to call BeginTerminal before writeTerminal in its own body, and it looks names up only in session_terminal.go and usage.go. After the extraction `cascadeToChildren` calls neither, so CODE-2 now stages the list edit (cancelNodeGuarded in place of cascadeToChildren, plus failBudgetKeysExpired, with the lookup also reading budget_keys_expired.go) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_writer_inventory_test.go:85, :250-270, :315-327. 0105 adds failStarting and 0106 removes expireSession from the same list, so the edit is stated relative to those changes.
WATCHOUT: `OpenTerminalWork()` counts every claim in the record, so after a refused-root detection it is not 0 at handler return: the sweep's detached descendant hooks still hold their claims (and join the usage quiescence wait). Assert "the root holds no terminal claim" there, and assert 0 only for the store-error case, where no sweep runs — EVIDENCE: sessionserver.go:1931-1935; usage.go:781-791.
MISTAKE: an earlier round wrote "`OpenTerminalWork()` is 0 when the handler returns" for the refused root, citing rule 2, which is the rule that makes the claim false. It cost a finding with six lenses.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]

The watchdog and orphan rows now read "detached, by that row" with Changed by "—", the Sequencing sentence crediting 0105 CODE-4 item 2 is deleted, and the summary's 0105 impact row is cut to a file-overlap note — BECAUSE `OnSessionTerminal` calls `recordSessionCompleted` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:255-257), which 0107 detaches, and `recordCompleted` (watchdog.go:1589), `failOrphan` (orphansession.go:394), and `endOrphan` (orphancleanup.go:270) reach the hook only through `OnSessionTerminal`. So under 0107 alone no sweep pass waits on the quiescence wait. 0105 CODE-4 item 2 detaches only the outer call — ALTERNATIVES: dropping 0105 from D15 and Sequencing was rejected because the file overlap below is real; saying both proposals detach keeps the rule in three places.
FACT: the 0105/0107 file overlap that keeps 0105 in D15 is this. 0105 CODE-6 Targets name `usage.go` (`isPreRunningClaimState`) and `session_terminal.go` (`failStarting`), which 0107 CODE-3 also edits. 0105 CODE-7 edits `delegation/service.go` (the `ErrParentNotRunning` admission row) and `mcptools_register.go`, which 0107 CODE-4 also edits — EVIDENCE: proposals/0105_*/0105_*.non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 Targets (:114), CODE-7 (:148, :180).
CORRECTS [the earlier DECISION crediting 0105 CODE-4 item 2 with the watchdog and orphan detachment]: that reliance was false. The claim cell "own, ended when `OnSessionTerminal` returns" holds with or without 0105, because 0107 detaches inside `OnSessionTerminal` for every caller.
OPEN: the CODE-3 item 6 writer table has no row for 0105 CODE-6 item 3's `failStarting`. It is a new hook-path writer in session_terminal.go that runs `afterFailed` and should be inline, like `failFinalizing`. This was left for a later round as a separate finding (0105 non-spec-changes :135).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED root write reaches the hook through `s.OnSessionTerminal(ctx, from, updated)` and keeps Dispatch "detached" and Claim "own, handed to the goroutine" in its CODE-3 item 6 row. Only the "Reaches the hook through" cell changes — BECAUSE `afterFailed` reaches neither terminalSignals (§16.6 event, §14 callback via enqueueTerminalCallback, watermark, cascade) nor afterTerminalRelease (session log, quota checkpoint, §11.2.1 billing). S11 (CODE-2) lands before S15 (CODE-3), and CODE-3 item 6 is what makes OnSessionTerminal detached, so at S11 OnSessionTerminal is still synchronous. If the writer called it inline, the handler would block on the root's seal before the sweep, and Testing item 2's held-seal bullet would fail at S11 — ALTERNATIVES: "detached, by that row; own, ended when OnSessionTerminal returns" (clean only after S15, and S11→S15→S13→S11 is a cycle, so it cannot be reordered); adding enqueueTerminalCallback and billing to settledChildSignals or a new tail (a parallel pipeline, which is hair).
WATCHOUT: after S15 the root write's goroutine wraps an already-detached OnSessionTerminal. That is harmless, because the writer's claim ends when OnSessionTerminal returns and OnSessionTerminal's own claim covers the hook. Do not "simplify" it to an inline call unless the checklist order changes — EVIDENCE: implementation-checklist.md S11/S13/S15 dependencies; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:255 (OnSessionTerminal is synchronous in the tree)
FACT: with the funnel route, the root's own terminalSignals runs cascadeToChildren under the root's cascadeOnFailure policy, concurrently with the CODE-2 3(b) sweep. Both open BeginTerminal and use nonTerminalGuard, so the loser gets ErrSuperseded. ### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]

DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings in non-spec round 1 of the fourth run. BECAUSE the spec-r3-prefix diff only adds 0105 to Sequencing and D15 and rewords two SPEC-4/SPEC-5 lines, so it adds no store write, watch, key, or goroutine. The whole-document load profile matches the earlier passes: one goroutine plus one refcounted terminal claim per delegated-child terminal, held for at most the quiescence timeout; SubtreeTokens repeating the per-child TreeUsage reads on the read pool; CODE-8 issuing 2 primary ANY(500) queries per 500 tracked ids per replica per 60 s (about 20 qps fleet-wide at 10k tracked ids x 30 replicas); and nothing on etcd. ALTERNATIVES: (1) I dropped filing CODE-7 item 4's Deny as a call that could run under the coordinator-wide lock of item 3, because the text does not put it under the lock and the lock-free order (Deny, then lock, clear pending, close done) satisfies both items. (2) I dropped filing the parallelChildren slot held about 5 s, because it is settled under D3 and R12.
FACT: the funnel's inline hook from ReportAttachStreamFailure runs on a per-stream reader goroutine, and the resume driver's reportResumeFailure runs per session (attemptResume). So the 5 s join in an inline writer stalls no serial multi-session loop. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/stream_failure.go:46-47; resume_driver.go:134-168, :352-358
FACT: terminalContext is WithoutCancel(ctx) bounded by seal window + release allowance from TerminatedAt, and the chart default seal window is 300 s (0 means 300). The startup gate (timeout < seal window) therefore affects only the kind overlay that CODE-3 already edits. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2000-2006; charts/lenny/values.yaml:2288; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:475
WATCHOUT: under CODE-7 item 3's single coordinator lock, any store I/O inside the critical section serializes every tree on the replica, and during a Postgres failover it stalls them, because the episode context is Background. This applies to Deny (item 4) as well as resolveSuccessCoolOff. Today Deny runs after tc.mu.Unlock. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/elicitation.go:146-167

### [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]

WATCHOUT: `OpenTerminalWork()` counts every session with an open claim, descendants included. ### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]

FACT: SubtreeTokens doubles an existing per-terminal read rather than adding a new pattern. TreeUsage already runs ListByRoot plus treeArchive.Replay on the root for every settled child (usage.go:929, taskrecord.go:69). With the default maxTreeSize of 50 (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:125, :257), a full-tree cascade reads at most about 2,500 rows per read path, against the read replica. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/resultrollup/resultrollup.go:123-141
FACT: Detaching OnSessionTerminal for the §12.8 drains matches the spec. §12.8 **Lifecycle fence and drain** states "A successful drain does not wait for the session's terminal steps or the release of its pod." The concurrent seal burst is bounded per replica, because the seal step skips the Sealer when there is no local binding, so the cap is maxSessionsPerReplica (400 at Tier 3), the same burst §10.1.8 budgets. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:812; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:189
FACT: 0105 CODE-4 item 2 already hands the watchdog, orphan cleanup, and orphan reconciler hooks to per-row goroutines, so 0107 adds no new burst on those writers. EVIDENCE: proposals/0105_*/0105_*non-spec-changes.md:100
FACT: StoreBudgetSource.TreeBudget, with its row, root, parent, Denied, and Grants reads, is called only from the extension paths (budgetextension.go:120, leasecontrol.go:820). It is not on the per-LLM-request hot path. The CODE-8 sweep issues about 2 queries per minute per replica at Tier 3 (one 500-id batch).
FACT: The shipped returnTreeBudget already discards Return errors and never retries (usage.go:603-613), so CODE-3 item 4's leak-on-error degrades no further on a Redis outage or a Postgres failover. A SubtreeTokens error yields TokenDelta 0, which leaves the slice counted.
USEFUL [Settled: Perf, Read replicas, Cut return accepted]: these saved re-deriving the write-rate and failover analysis.
UNVERIFIED: whether Testing item 2's clause "the root holds no terminal claim when the handler returns" applies only to the recovery-state roots. For a failed root, rule 2 has OnSessionTerminal's detached claim still counted on the root at handler return. A test-coverage lens should read it. It is outside this lens and I did not file it.
